@@126531008 <p> As a scholar of law , sociology , and history , Dr Elsa van Huyssteen should be well aware of the concept of lawfare : the strategic use of legal instruments to achieve political aims , often in tandem with military or public relations strategies . Lawfare does not openly declare its political objectives -- that would defeat its purpose . Instead , it cloaks itself in virtuous language , masking private or ideological interests under the guise of legal or moral propriety . A key aim is to exhaust its targets by entangling them in expensive and time-consuming legal proceedings over frivolous or vexatious claims . This is often aligned with SLAPP suits ( Strategic Lawsuits Against Public Participation ) , used globally to silence human rights defenders . <p> The pro-Israel lobby has actively engaged in lawfare as a strategic pillar , mobilizing legal teams , and a war chest of funding , to stifle pro-Palestinian advocacy around the world , and increasingly so after October 2023 . <p> Van Huyssteen 's defence of Professor Adam Mendelsohn and her critique of Kevin Bloom 's article in the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ unrelated to Zionism or the war in Gaza -- is disingenuous . She merely echoes Mendelsohn 's claim in his affidavit that " this case has very little to do with Israel and the legality of its conduct in Gaza . " <p> Assessing such a case without context is foolish , as it leads to false conclusions , as in this case . <p> Mendelsohn 's actions betray his objective : to suppress criticism of Israel and Zionism , and to obstruct solidarity efforts with Palestinians . His legal efforts , supplemented by other strategies , are supported by a cohort of largely white academics at UCT , most of whom consistently oppose transformation efforts at the institution . Notably , during the early stages of Israel 's war on Gaza , the UCT Senate struggled even to pass a basic resolution calling for a humanitarian ceasefire -- a motion that should have been universally supported on humanitarian grounds . <p> --&gt; <p> Mendelsohn 's stated concern for " governance , sustainability , and academic freedom " is belied by his selective engagement . @@126572104 <h> OPINION <h> Operation Rising Lion in its broader context <p> Mike Berger <p> 02 July 2025 <p> Mike Berger reflects on the significance of the twelve day Israel-Iran war <p> The following article is taken intact from an earlier Substack post of mine but omitting certain paragraphs which were rendered moot by the American strike on the Iranian nuclear sites , just as it went out . With that the war essentially ended with a few last minutes flurries , mainly symbolic from the Iranians . As expected they claimed victory , but it was a resounding military defeat which in the short-term has transformed the balance of forces in the ME . <p> The events and the military accomplishments of Israel since 7 October 2023 will be stuff of history perhaps for centuries to come . But remarkable though these are the war against Jihadist extremism in the region is not over ; nor is it the whole story . New alliances and coalitions are brewing as we speak and the region by virtue of its geography , history and clashing ideologies will be a fount of global @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ <p> *** <p> On Friday , 13 June Israel initiated Operation ' Rising Lion ' with a combination of intelligence-driven air assaults and ground operations that within a few day had incapacitated much of Iran 's nuclear capacity at the Natanz and Isfahan plants and elsewhere , decapitated the top political , military and intelligence leadership of the IRGC , eliminated at least 9 of her leading nuclear scientists and destroyed the major part of her missile launchers and air defense systems . <p> Since then the war has continued with Israel striking strategic targets while protecting civilians as far as possible and leaving most of Iran 's economic foundations intact . When Iran recovered somewhat from the initial strike it responded with missiles and drones . Most of these were intercepted but some did get through inflicting civilian and infrastructural damage in built-up areas . It appears that Iran has focussed mainly on civilian areas with relatively minor damage to major strategic assets . <p> Israel knows its enemies by now and has built a remarkable system of shelters which has mitigated their worst intentions so far . But @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ been using hypersonic , ballistic missiles , some armed with cluster bombs thus spreading the target area indiscriminately . There has been no international reaction to the use of such munitions which may constitute a war crime . Given the double standard ruthlessly applied to Israel , such silence is not unexpected . <p> --&gt; <p> Apart from the war in the ME , the propaganda war has been in full swing especially in Western democracies . This involves the usual Islamic street marches and protest theatre including the always present threat of violence . So far that been largely contained but the deliberately reckless and false reporting by the likes of The Guardian , the BBC and the NYT ( just to mention the most prominent ) continues unabated - and unabashed . <p> The official sources are of course abetted by social media influencers of all kinds spreading violence porn and fakes indiscriminately . In all this the malign strategy of Hamas to use its civilian population as propaganda fodder is completely obscured . Even more insidious , its clearly and repeatedly expressed intention of exterminating the Israeli @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ an oppressed people . The same selective blindness being applied to Hamas is carried over to Iran , indeed to any polity which threatens the existence of Israel . <p> I return to this theme later , but it needs to be acknowledged that despite the anti-Israel furore the centre continues to hold . Individuals like John Spencer , Andrew Fox , Richard Kemp , Douglas Murray , Hugh Hewitt and many others from outside the Jewish community have stood firm . This includes platforms like The Free Press . <p> And of course , defenders from within the Jewish community have tried to bring truth and reason to the debate while respecting the democratic norms And here lies the fundamental question : can democratic values survive the internal and external challenges of the rapidly globalising world ? This is the fundamental political issue of our time . <p> --&gt; <p> How have we reached this point ? <p> As the world shrinks under the unstoppable impact of a globalising technological revolution , disruption in one region rapidly spreads to all quarters of the globe . Just over a century @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ the final collapse of an ailing Ottoman Empire under the onslaught of an expansionist , technologically and politically advanced Western civilisation . <p> This was followed by the humiliation of being divided up into spheres of influence by the victors , and further compounded by the influx of European Jews driven chiefly by genocidal European anti--Semitism and a Jewish redemptive movement which came to be called Zionism . The discovery of oil ( not in Israel ) and the strategic significance of the region for global trade raised the stakes considerably . <p> Inevitably this became an arena of violent contestation involving local actors and their global sponsors and handlers . It spawned a host of regional political movements ranging from secular to apocalyptic religious . The latter , in the form of the radical Shi'itism of the current Iranian regime and the Sunni-based Muslin Brotherhood , are central to the present narrative . <p> --&gt; <p> The early history is complex and contested . The initial local Arab-Muslim response to the Jewish influx into the Levant was mixed , varying from welcoming the economic opportunity it provided to resentment @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ themselves were not homogeneous . Some sought to cultivate Arab acceptance and worked hard towards mutual cooperation whereas ' realists ' foresaw the likelihood of on-going conflict . The UN mandated British authority in charge was erratic , earning ultimately the distrust and enmity of both sides . <p> Mohammed Amin al-Husseini , from the prominent al-Husayni Jerusalem family , was appointed the Grand Mufti of Jerusalem in 1921 by the British High Commissioner of the time . In that position he swung the tide irreversibly against Arab-Jewish co-existence in Palestine . Over the next one and a half decades he instigated a number of bloody anti-Jewish ( and anti-British ) riots . He ultimately fled in 1937 to Europe where he allied himself to the Nazi movement . <p> But the die had been cast . By the time Israeli Independence had been declared in 1948 and the pan-Arab war against the new State had failed , most of the Arab-Muslim movements were radicalised and united against the small Jewish presence in the ME . In the subsequent era , the ' Palestinians ' emerged as a focus of @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Palestinian ' in quotes since they were an artificial , but strategically useful , national identity created from the diverse group of Arab-Muslims resident in the Palestine of 1948 - indistinguishable from the Arab-Muslim populations surrounding them . <p> --&gt; <p> There never was a Palestinian State or a Palestinian People . Ever since the destruction of the Second Temple in the year 70 of the Common Era followed by dispersal of the Jews , the Levant had been occupied by various contending forces competing for dominance until it became a province of the Ottoman Empire in the 16th century . This period of relative stability came to an end with the collapse of the Ottomans following WW1 and the temporary rule of Western colonial powers . <p> But the ' Palestinians ' , like the Jews , are now part of history and a way of satisfying their new national identity will ultimately need to be found - if possible . The truth is they 're an anomaly . Elevated by circumstance and strategic calculations to an ersatz importance they divert attention from the real issues in the ME @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ fall ) over the past nearly 5 decades of the apocalyptic religious radicalism of the Ayatollah 's regime in Iran , which is the subject of the next section . <p> Apocalyptic Radicalism in Islam and the role of Iran <p> In this section I 'm especially indebted to the remarkable book ' Heaven on Earth : The varieties of the Millennial Experience ' by Richard Landes . It is available free as a PDF file and I strongly recommend it . It 's not written as a popular best-seller or a polemic but will repay the specialist and interested amateur alike . <p> As I understand Landes , millennial thinking is a form of extreme Utopianism linked often to specific dates , prophets and events . It often ( not always ) contains apocalyptic elements and divides the world into the aware and the blind , or the actively oppositional and evil . It cuts across eras and cultures and may be secular or religious , passive or active . He provides 14 examples ranging from the Xhosa Cattle-Slaying to the French Revolution to Nazi Paranoia to the recent @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ in situations of chaos , change and heightened tension . It offers a radical critique of the current predicament and possible responses . My interest here is in his final chapter of ' Enraged Millennialism : Global Jihad ' precipitated by the failure of Islam in the face of Western modernity . <p> I can not capture in a short article the full depth and subtlety of Landes 's analysis but will focus on the rise of radical Jihadism in its various forms over the past century or so following the collapse of the Ottoman Empire . Here is my interpretation of his core propositions , some of which I touched on earlier in this article : <p> 1 . The last century has been a source of enormous pain , confusion and humiliation for Islam . There have been various responses ranging from embracing modernism , adjusting to it or active opposition of various types . A focal point for many has been the ability of the Jews , previously a lowly dhimmi people ( protected unbelievers ) , to establish themselves as a presence in the midst of @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ apocalyptic movements sprung up which elevated the Jews to a cosmically evil force ( the dajjal or antichrist ) capable of bending whole nations to their will . Some of the these narratives relied heavily on the Protocols of Zion . Such extreme narratives though dismissed by some , possibly most Muslims , were useful in energising the impressionable masses . <p> 3 . The two most apocalyptically religious movements were the radical Shiite Ayatollahs in Iran and the various offshoots of Sunni Muslim Brotherhood , notably Hamas in Gaza . Both incorporated elements of Nazi fascism , Marxian socialism and the victimhood narratives of the radical Progressive Left in their public utterances and PR campaigns . <p> 4 . But to mobilise their own people to make the necessary sacrifices and commitment , at the core of their teaching is the belief that Jihad and Martyrdom are the fundamental demands of Allah for entry to Heaven and its delights . <p> 5 . What the postmodern Left sees as prime virtues - the equality and freedom of women , the open expression of sexuality , the destruction of the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ the elevation of the individual over the collective , egalitarianism - elicit contempt and disgust in the devout follower . <p> Some idea of the most extreme expressions of this apocalyptic vision are captured in the following quotes from Landes : <p> " This new apocalypticism was , accordingly , remorselessly cataclysmic and paranoid . The forces of evil that conspired against God 's chosen were everywhere , and everywhere evil . Muslim apocalyptic writings embraced any and every conspiracy theory , every fevered dread of annihilation , every envy-ridden and rage-soaked hatred that millennial visions can , at their worst , produce . The enemy , these writers assumed , ... was cosmically evil , inhuman , diabolical. , , Nothing was too base or too evil for this enemy -- human sacrifice , demonic plots , genocide . " <p> " The role of the Islamic stream is to keep the flame of hatred toward Zionism burning in their souls . This is because we are not ready to fight and use our military power , due to the limitations forced on us . We are not capable @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ because it is not in our hand , but rather in the hands of others meaning , the Palestinian Authority . Nevertheless , we are capable of cultivating the flame of hatred to this enemy in the souls of our sons , daughters , and grandchildren . We can make hatred burn among the public . If we manage to do so , in our homes and with the help of our schools and media , our efforts will be successful . The fighting will come one of these days and if by that time the ideology of hatred has faded , we will be defeated ; on the other hand , if on this day we will still hate Israel , victory will be ours , with the help of Allah . " <p> " If we kill infidels in order to put a stop to their corrupting activities , we have indeed done them a service . . . To allow the infidels to stay alive means to let them do more corrupting activities . To kill them is a surgical operation commanded by Allah . . . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ nation . " ( Khomeini ) <p> " By virtue of the distribution of Muslims , who pursue the cause of the Hamas , all over the globe , and strive for its victory , for the reinforcement of its positions and for the encouragement of its Jihad , the Movement is a universal one . . . Whoever denigrates its worth , or avoids supporting it , or is so blind as to dismiss its role , is challenging Fate itself , , , " . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ from Islamic sources ( other than the first quote which is from Landes ) . They capture , in small part the mindset driving the Jihadist movements in the ME and globally . The idea that compromise and the attractions of a deal will work against the true believers in the " Axis of Resistance " is pure delusion ( or serious ignorance ) . Unambiguous defeat and re-education is the minimum for long-term release of the ME and the world from the endless cycle of violence and conquest envisaged by Jihadist Islam . <p> What accounts for the embrace of this fanatical movement by significant segments of the West ? <p> In a short but brilliant analysis Adam Louis-Klein sets the current conflict in the history of European anti-Jewish pogroms from the medieval blood libels onwards . They did not abate with modernity , the Enlightenment and the emancipation of the Jews , but mutated into new forms and different justifications . <p> Ultimately they culminated in the Holocaust which , in theory , should have immunised modern populations against the demonic , magical thinking which drove the Nazi @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ tropes and patterns emerged waiting for some obsessed , apocalyptic prophet to give voice to the hatreds , envies , lusts and fears circulating in society . <p> Even worse , they were transplanted to the ME where they fused with radical Islamic theologies , then re-imported back into the European theatre where they again merged with the endogenous progressive ' **27;1534;TOOLONG justice ' millennial project . <p> Clearly the ease with which such thought patterns take root in society point to deep-seated vulnerabilities in the human psyche to scripts containing cosmic villains . The two millennia-long narrative of Jewish evil provides a well-travelled path to apocalyptic fantasies . But it required sophisticated , money-fuelled propaganda and the complicity of educated elites and their institutions to overcome the sheer unbelievability of a tiny thriving Israeli democracy wilfully undertaking the genocide and oppression of its 350 million , oil-rich neighbours . <p> Somehow also the sadistic atrocities of 7 October 2023 had to be explained away along with the 500+ km of well-stocked and armed tunnels for Hamas fighters under homes , schools , hospitals and mosques . The total absence @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ evidence of the ' ring of fire ' prepared by Iran for just this eventuality had to be ignored . At the same time there needed to be instantaneous and wholly uncritical acceptance of every outlandish claim made by Hamas spokespersons . <p> The visceral urge to believe over-powered all critical and moral faculties in otherwise well-educated Western individuals . Whence did that diabolical impulse come from ? <p> On the answer , or rather answers , to this question hangs the fate not only of Israel but on the sustainability of democracy in diverse societies facing the challenges of external enemies . This issue can not be further explored here but suffice it to say it will always require resolve and clarity of thought in the face of rumour , hysteria , passing fashions and magical thinking . 
@@126599086 <h> The Hague Group revives the possibility of a new internationalism <p> A vigil remembering journalists killed in Palestine held at St.Georges Cathedral in Cape Town on 28 January 2024 . Photo by Leanne Brady <p> Imperialism is on a genocidal offensive in Gaza , and has recently bombed its way through Syria , Yemen , Lebanon and Iran too . But there is also a growing international pushback . In the West , young people are standing with Palestine in growing numbers . <p> In Africa , the rise of anti-imperialist sentiment across West Africa and the Sahel marks one of the most significant political shifts in a generation . In Mali , Niger , Burkina Faso , and now Senegal , we are witnessing a rupture with the neocolonial order -- a rejection of French military presence , International Monetary Fund tutelage and elite subservience to Western interests . The emergence of figures such as Captain Ibrahim Traor ? in Burkina Faso reflects a desire to reclaim sovereignty not in speeches , but through concrete acts of defiance . <p> While these new formations are uneven and at @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ the postcolonial consensus . There is a new assertion of a politics rooted in self-determination , resistance to external domination , and the urgent need to build new regional solidarities on African terms . <p> South Africa has , in a very different way , led a hugely important challenge to the West by taking Israel to the International Court of Justice and then , in January this year , taking a leading role in the formation of The Hague Group , a coalition of Global South nations formed in response to Israel 's genocidal attack on Gaza and committed to upholding international law and confronting impunity . <p> Nine nations -- Belize , Bolivia , Colombia , Cuba , Honduras , Malaysia , Namibia , Senegal , and South Africa -- came together to commit to upholding the provisional measures of the International Court of Justice , which found plausible evidence that Israel is committing genocide in Gaza . They pledged to prevent arms transfers that could contribute to the genocide , to block military cargo from docking at their ports , and to begin building the legal and @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ abetting war crimes . <p> For the first time in decades , the postcolonial world stood together and spoke in a voice not dictated by Washington , Tel Aviv or Brussels . The presence of our government among those leading the charge signals a meaningful rupture with the status quo -- a reminder that , at its best , the ANC tradition carries within it the embers of a once-vibrant internationalism . In standing against genocide , and in asserting the illegality of occupation and collective punishment in Palestine , the South African state returned to its historic alignment with the oppressed of the world . <p> Internationalism has never been a luxury for the oppressed . It is a necessity . Our own struggle against apartheid was not won through domestic action alone . It was carried on the shoulders of Cuban brigades , Swedish trade unionists , Caribbean intellectuals and Indian feminists . It was amplified by the voices of Latin American and African writers . It was supported by movements across the world that understood that apartheid was not simply a local aberration , but a node @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ same is true of Palestine today . To isolate what is happening in Gaza from the broader structures of occupation , imperialism and settler colonialism is to misunderstand its function in the world system . Palestine is not simply a humanitarian crisis -- it is a political one . Its resolution will not come through diplomacy alone , but through the reconstruction of international solidarity as a material force . <p> In July , the Hague Group will reconvene in Bogot ? , co-hosted by South Africa and Colombia , to deepen these commitments . Discussions will focus on expanding the group , developing regional enforcement mechanisms and coordinating legal and trade responses to states and corporations complicit in Israeli apartheid . <p> The growing power of the Hague Group gestures back toward Bandung and the Non-Aligned Movement -- not as nostalgic artefacts , but as unfinished projects : projects that sought to create an alternative axis of power among the formerly colonised . <p> Internationalism in Africa has deep roots . Kwame Nkrumah , in his speech to the Conference of Independent African States in 1960 , declared : @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ linked up with the total liberation of the African continent . " His vision of a pan-African future was inseparable from a global anti-imperialist alignment . In 1961 , he helped launch the Casablanca Group , laying the foundations for continental unity rooted in anti-colonial solidarity . <p> Thomas Sankara insisted that internationalism without class politics is hollow . Speaking at the Organisation of African Unity summit in 1987 , just months before his assassination , he said : " The debt problem is a neo-colonial plot . We can not repay the debt because we are not responsible for it . Instead , we must build solidarity among the peoples of the South to end this global system of plunder . " <p> Today , Africa faces the same global forces that Nkrumah and Sankara named : imperial domination , predatory finance and extractive trade arrangements . What has changed is the scale of the crisis -- from climate collapse to mass displacement -- and the growing realisation that no national project can resolve these challenges in isolation . A renewed African internationalism must begin from this understanding : @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ history , but by necessity . <p> Internationalism is not just about states . It is also about class . Gaza , like Marikana , like the imperialist stranglehold on Haiti , the brutal monarchy in Swaziland and the imperialist driven war in the Democratic Republic of the Congo , is nested into the system of global capital -- a system that profits from weapons , from land theft , from forced displacement , from cheap labour . It is a system that organises violence through borders and banks , and calls it civilisation . To truly confront it , solidarity can not be abstract . It must be rooted in the lived realities of working people -- their dispossession , exploitation and resistance . <p> This means reimagining our role in the world -- not as supplicants or junior partners , but as protagonists of a different future . A future in which justice is not calibrated according to proximity to power , but according to our shared histories of resistance . <p> When the Hague Group was formed in January this year , it immediately won the support @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ often divided South African left . There was a universal recognition that this was a critical moment and one that all of us , including those of us with very strong criticisms of the ANC , must support . We will offer the same support to the meeting in Bogot ? . <p> There is now a rare opportunity to begin rebuilding forms of southern multilateralism that are not captured by elite diplomacy or reduced to symbolic performance . This will require not only coordination between states , but active connection with movements , unions , and civic organisations rooted in material struggle . Without a real grounding in democratic forces below the level of state power , any new alignment risks becoming yet another site of compromise and betrayal . <p> The Hague Group is a critical step forward . It has shown that coordinated action by states in the Global South is possible -- serious , principled and effective . But if this moment is to mark the beginning of a new internationalism , not just a high point of symbolic clarity , then its scope must expand @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ now be extended to the people of Swaziland , the DRC , Western Sahara and elsewhere -- places where impunity still rules , and where silence has too often passed for diplomacy . The work of justice does not end in Gaza . It begins wherever brutal force is used to foreclose the future of the oppressed -- and it must be carried forward with the same unity , courage , and clarity of purpose . <p> Mbuso Ngubane is the deputy general secretary of the National Union of Metalworkers of South Africa and writes in his personal capacity .

She underlined that the event presented an opportunity to draw attention to the dire water crisis in Gaza , where millions of Palestinians have been denied access to clean water for more than two years , and reiterated the Palestinian people 's right to peace and self-determination . <p> The programme included speeches by Gugelethu Zwane , deputy director-general for institutional planning and support of the department of science , technology and innovation , as well as by Moses Tembo , of the African Development Bank in Southern Africa . <p> They both spoke about the efforts made in water management and reiterated the importance of enhancing African cooperation in this field . <p> A panel discussion was dedicated to the scientific research and cooperation between Tunisia and South Africa on water management . @@126673369 <h> Why are we so negative ? An expert answers <h> Why do we forget the congratulations but remember the insult ? <p> Doomscrolling is one of the ways we fill up on negativity . Picture : Keasha Werner <p> Why is it that , while we all want to see the glass as half full , we tend to see it as half empty ? Why do we forget the congratulations but remember the insult ? What is it about the human condition that makes us default to the negative ? <p> Even if all humans were chocolate cupcakes , the salted caramel icing may taste more snarky than delectable at times . It 's called negativity bias , and it seems to be baked into the fibre of who we are as people . <p> " In ancient times , noticing a rustle in the bushes could have meant a predator was nearby , ready to eat us , " he said . " That kind of vigilance helped our ancestors survive . But now the same reaction kicks in when someone ignores your message on WhatsApp or @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Stuart Soroka , Patrick Fournier and Lilach Nir studied audiences across 17 countries and found that people consistently paid more attention to negative news than to positive stories . <p> Their research measured physical and emotional reactions to video news content and revealed a global pattern that human beings are simply more aroused by negativity . <p> " This is why doomscrolling on devices exists , " said Dr Redelinghuys . " It 's not that the world has become more terrible . It 's that we are more likely to notice and believe the terrible parts of it . " <p> Right now , people have a myriad of options to scroll to , like the Israel , Iran , Gaza conflict , Ukraine and Russia , Cash in Transit Heists in Mzansi , and thieving politicians . <p> " It 's a feast of negativity made accessible by the internet and its platforms , " said Dr Redelinghuys . <h> We absorb negativity easier <p> The problem , he said , is that we do n't just see the stuff , we absorb it . <p> " It @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ and the way we relate to other people , " he said . <p> " A boss berating you at work sticks with you longer than a kind word from the same person . A failed job interview might haunt you for weeks . One strange look or word from someone else can ruin a perfectly decent day . " <p> " After that , you can give someone all the validation in the world , but it 's the one piece of criticism they remember . That one moment becomes the headline in their thoughts . <p> Online forums are full of people feeling challenged with this exact experience . <p> One user on Reddit wrote , " I know when something is objectively fine , but my emotions just wo n't accept it . I live in this loop of expecting things to go wrong . " <p> Another shared , " I 've turned my life around completely , but I still only see the failures . It 's like I only know how to function through negativity . " <p> It 's not easy to shut @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ potholes of negative bias . <p> " You do n't silence it completely , " said Dr Redelinghuys . " But you can learn to notice it for what it is and stop letting it control the narrative of your life . " <h> Mindfulness can help <p> Mindfulness can work . <p> A study by researchers Kiken and Shook in 2011 found that people who practised mindful breathing became more aware of positive experiences and developed more optimistic attitudes . <p> Other tools include journaling , reframing and making a conscious effort to notice the good things when they happen . <p> " It 's not about pretending everything is perfect , " noted Dr Redelinghuys . " It 's about giving positive moments a fair chance to land . The brain is already keeping score of everything that went wrong . Balance that out . " <p> Cognitive restructuring is a big term , but an easier implementation . This is where you actively challenge negative thoughts , consciously , and replace them with more balanced notions . <p> Another simple idea , Dr Redelinghuys suggested , is @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ <p> " Take a moment to fully enjoy the meal , the compliment , the peaceful evening . Let it register , " he suggested . <p> " You do not have to be blindly optimistic . But you can stop treating the negative as the only truth worth knowing fully . "
