@@126711605 <p> 1 . I am a criminal justice consultant and I work remotely from Cape Town , London and Miami . I have a longstanding relationship with the University of Cape Town . I am an alumna of the Universities of Cape Town and Cambridge . I lectured Criminal Law and Criminology courses at the Kramer Law Faculty at the University of Cape Town for 18 years until April 2023 . I was elected by the Convocation to serve on the Council from 1 July 2024 for a four year term . <p> 2 . I am the 27th respondent in this application . I am cited in this application in my capacity as a member of the Council of the University of Cape Town ( " the Council " ) . <p> 3 . I abide by the decision of this Court , as indicated in my Notice of Intention to Abide dated 6 September 2024 . <p> --&gt; <p> 4 . The facts set out in this affidavit fall within my personal knowledge , unless the context indicates otherwise . I believe these facts to be true and correct . Where information does not fall within my personal knowledge , but arises from documents that I have considered , I refer to those documents in this affidavit . <p> 5 . Certain of the documents I have had to refer to include Council documents and deliberations deemed confidential according to the Council 's confidentiality regime . In so doing , I believe I am acting in accordance with the UCT Council Code of Conduct ( passed in terms of S 27(7E) ( a ) of the Higher Education Act , Act 101 of 1997 as amended ) : <p> " Council members undertake collectively and individually to ... maintain the confidentiality of Council deliberations , and of all proprietary , strategic and sensitive or valuable information of the University entrusted to them , exceptwhen disclosure is authorised or legally mandated . " <p> 6 . I have read the 26th Respondent 's explanatory affidavit and confirm all content therein as it pertains to me . Rather than unnecessarily increasing the workload of the court , where the 26th Respondent makes averments that would have been identical to my own , I confirm these specifically below and only expand on matters on which I believe my experience and perspective adds additional value . <p> 7 . My affidavit comprises of the following sections : <p> A. Background and reason for deposing this affidavit <p> B. Council meeting 27 July 2024 ; <p> The Retention of Audio Recordings of Council Meetings <p> C. Build up to and Special Council meeting of 5 October 2024 Receipt of proceedings <p> Letter of 5 September 2024 <p> Events leading to 5 October Meeting Special Meeting 5 October 2024 <p> D. Council meeting of 19 October 2024 and related events Ad Hoe Committee on Non-Disclosure <p> Audio recordings of Council meetings VC Report 19 October Meeting <p> E. Events of 2025 <p> Council Meeting 15 March 2025 <p> Special meeting of 12 June 2025 <p> F. Conclusion <p> A. BACKGROUND AND REASON FOR DEPOSING THIS AFFIDAVIT <p> 8 . I was not present at the Council meeting of 22 June 2024 , where the impugned resolutions were passed . However , I can speak to subsequent meetings and events raised in the papers before this Court . I can also speak to explanations given in those meetings that are pertinent to understanding the circumstances in which decisions in the June 22 meeting were made , insofar as they have been put before this Court by others . ( cf para 567 of the Answering Affidavit deposed to by the Second Respondent , Adv Norman Arendse SC , Chair of the Council ( " the Chair " ) ) . <p> 9 . I did not have the opportunity to contribute to the affidavits deposed to by the Chair and other members on behalf of Council . In fact , I did not see the affidavit until after it was filed . Council is a council of equals , the Chair is a chair amongst equals . He is not elevated above other council members . Deposing on Council 's behalf did not relieve him of the duty to adequately consult . <p> 10 . I am advised that , where misrepresentations and false statements have been placed before the Court , as a member of a public body I have a duty of candour to assist the Court to be fully apprised of the facts . <p> 11 . At this juncture , I note that it has been suggested that I am " entering the fray " by filing this affidavit , that I am aligning with the relief sought by the Applicant and that I should therefore join as a co-Applicant and withdraw my notice to abide . <p> 11.1 . On 20 May 2025 , after receipt of the Answering Affidavit through our respective attorneys , the 26th Respondent wrote to the Interim Registrar on behalf of us both to request the records of certain Council meetings , saying the following : <p> " Kelly and I have considered the answering affidavit filed by the Chair of Council together with the supporting and confirmatory affidavits filed . We are concerned that certain of the facts alleged by the Chair in his answering affidavit on behalf of Council are inconsistent with our recollection of what was said in Council meetings . As members of Council , and repositories of public power under the HEA and UCT 's Statute , we have a duty to be candid with the Court regarding any and all facts that informed the impugned decision and the conduct of Council generally " . <p> 11.2 . Contrary to the averment made by the Chair in para 310.4 ( Answering Affidavit ) , on 23 May 2025 our request for Council documents was denied in a reply from Council 's legal representatives ( " Fairbridges " ) , resulting in the exchange of letters below ( attached as Annexure " KPH1 " ) : <p> " Your emails dated 20 May 2025 addressed to the Registrar Interim requesting certain documents have been forwarded to us to respond to . <p> The requests that you have submitted to the Registrar Interim indicate that you seek the documents and recordings in the context and for the purposes of the litigation . <p> You are required to comply with the Uniform Rules of Court to obtain documents and recordings for the purpose of the litigation , specifically rule 35 . This must be done via the attorneys on record for you . <p> Should you wish to enter the fray by requesting documents or <p> recordings and filing affidavits in the proceedings , you should withdraw your notices to abide and , if you support the relief Prof Mendelsohn is seeking , should join as co-applicants . <p> Please note that all queries relating to the litigation must be addressed to our offices and not to the Registrar Interim directly . " <p> 2 . We refer to your letter dated 23 May 2025 , as well as the minute of the case management meeting signed by all parties , dated 29 April 2025 . <p> 3 . We note your request that our client files a notice in terms of rule 35 of the Uniform Rules of Court to obtain materials from the Registrar of the University of Cape Town ( UCT ) concerning the meetings of Council , of which she is a member . The request appears to be informed by the suggestion that , in order for our client to file an affidavit , she should identify as an applicant and withdraw her notice to abide . <p> 4 . Our client rejects the suggestion that she aligns herself with the relief sought by the applicant . Our client also rejects the suggestion that she should join the proceedings as an applicant . <p> 5 . Our client is aware that a respondent may not support the relief of an applicant without joining as an applicant ( see African Transformation Movement v Speaker of the National Assembly 2023 3 All SA ( WCC ) para 77 ) . However , this rule does not apply to our client because she simply seeks to place material facts before the Court , in order to comply with her duty to the Court as a public litigant . <p> 6 . The fact that our client wishes to file an explanatory affidavit , dealing with certain factual averments , does not mean that she supports the relief of the applicant . <p> 7 . As you are aware , members of Council exercise statutory power when taking Council decisions . Its meetings are also statutorily regulated . Members of Council are public litigants with heightened constitutional obligations . They have a duty to be candid with the Court and not to mislead or obfuscate . As the Constitutional Court held in Public Protector v South African Reserve Bank 2019 ( 6 ) SA 253 ( CC ) para 152 : " 152 ... The Constitution requires public officials to be accountable and observe heightened standards in litigation . They must not mislead or obfuscate . They must do right and they must do it property . They are required to be candid and place a full and fair account of the facts before a court " . ( emphasis added ) <p> 8 . To the extent that there are factual averments made on behalf of Council that are inconsistent with the proceedings before Council , members have a duty to be candid with the Court about those facts . This is an obligation that the Constitution places on all repositories of public power , which includes Council members . <p> 9 . Preventing Council members from accessing information to which they are ordinarily entitled stymies their ability to fulfil their obligations to both the Court and to Council itself . <p> 10 . Legal advice provided to Council confirms that all members are entitled to records of the University , including all recordings of Council meetings , where the information is relevant and necessary to the discharge of their fiduciary obligations . There can be no doubt that , to the extent that there are omissions and/or misrepresentations concerning Council business , our client has a duty to the Court and UCT to correct any such misrepresentation(s) or omission(s) . <p> 11 . Our client is entitled to the information sought just as much as the Chair of Council is , and he is not elevated above other members of Council in accessing records . <p> 12 . You will accordingly appreciate that our client stands on a very different footing to a litigant who seeks to rely on rule 35 to obtain materials . Our client does not seek to obtain materials to advance any particular case , but to assist the Court as a public litigant , with facts arising from Council meetings . <p> 13 . Accordingly , our client sees no basis on which the Registrar may refuse to provide our client with the requested documents and recordings , nor any reason to cause unnecessary delays and expense to our client in filing a rule 35 . <p> 14 . We kindly request that your client reconsider its position and provide our client with the materials requested , to which she is entitled . <p> 15 . Our client reserves the right to place this correspondence before the Court in the event that it is necessary to do so . " <p> Fairbridges to Bowman 30 May 2025 : <p> " 1 . We refer to the matter of Mendelsohn I The Council of the University of Cape Town and Others and to your letter of 27 May 2025 . We do not respond to every averment in your letter under reply , and our failure to do so should not be construed as an admission of any of its contents . <p> 2 . Your letter confirms that your client seeks the requested documents and recordings " in order to place material facts before the Court " . Hence , the request is made in the context of and for the purpose of the litigation . <p> 3 . Our clients do not accept that your client " stands on a very different footing to a litigant who seeks to rely on rule 35 to obtain materials " . Your client is cited as a party to the litigation . She has purported to abide , although it appears that she seeks to enter the fray . <p> 4 . Our clients accordingly persist in contending that your client must invoke rule 35 to request documents or recordings for the purpose of the litigation . <p> 5 . Our clients also strongly refute and deny the suggestion in your letter that the Chairperson of Council has failed to place a full and fair account of the facts before the Court . <p> 6 . Our clients rights are reserved . " <p> 12 . I pause here to point out the oddity of the above reference to " our clients " . As members of Council , the 26th Respondent and I are in fact Fairbridges ' clients . Furthermore , as Council members we are entitled to Council materials in order to perform our fiduciary duties to UCT , which is what we were trying to do . <p> 13 . I did not reach the decision to depose this affidavit lightly . <p> 14 . It is false that I am " entering the fray " by filing this affidavit . I do not align myself with the relief sought by the applicant . I abide by the decision of this court . <p> 15 . I have consistently gone to great lengths to exercise my duties on Council constructively . The transcripts of every Council meeting I have participated in will back up my unitary focus on good governance on Council . Unfortunately , these efforts have been obstructed at almost every juncture , as illustrated throughout my affidavit . <p> 16 . I am also advised that in matters where a public body is divided the court should be apprised of the full spectrum of views . This matter has been highly contested on council . A number of votes have been taken and all of them have been close . It is not accurate to present an impression of consensus , as the Answering Affidavit does in para 672 with its reference to collective decision making and in para 12 . It is also not appropriate to give hearing in the court papers to only one side of those views , <p> 17 . Furthermore , it is misleading to depict subsequent decisions of Council and Convocation as having " endorsed " the 22 June Resolutions to the court ( see Answering Affidavit at para 11 ) , particularly in a context where council members have been silenced , as has been frequent . <p> 17.1 . Ad Answering Affidavit at para 11.1 - The Chair depicts a Convocation vote in favour of the impugned resolutions of 86 votes in support , 28 votes against , 12 abstentions , as endorsement . This is misleading . <p> 17.2 . First , I was at that meeting and I know how difficult it was for alumni to register to attend and vote . It was a protracted process and I was contacted by many alumni to complain that they either had not been notified of the meeting or could not successfully register . <p> 17.3 . Secondly , the convocation is comprised of all UCT graduates and holders of diplomas and credit-bearing certificates ; the vice-chancellor , the deputy vice ? chancellors and the current academic staff ; and all emeritus professors or emeritus associate professors . Just the alumni alone would make up many thousands of eligible voters - 86 votes in favour of the motion thus needs to be understood in this context . <p> 17.4 . The Notice of the AGM was sent out to 84 903 qualifying convocation members on the database and 482 accepted the invitation . Of that accepted figure , 387 indicated they will attend online and 97 were to be in person . On the day , 106 managed to join the meeting online and 61 were present . <p> 17.5 . Ad Answering Affidavit at paras 11.2.1 and 11.2.2 the chair refers to the Council votes on motions directed at the impugned resolutions on 27 July 2024 and 15 March 2025 respectively . He then concludes in para 12 that the impugned resolutions have " been endorsed by the newly-constituted Councif ' . <p> 17.6 . The votes referred to above were both contested and close - the resolutions survived suspension by 16 to 10 votes on 27 July , and the resolutions survived rescission by 14 to 13 votes with 1 abstention on 15 March . These slim margins can not be described as a resounding endorsement , neither can the debates at those meetings which led to them . <p> 18 . With the above in mind I proceed to set out the facts that I believe to be germane to this matter , which have not been adequately ventilated by the affidavits on behalf of Council . This is in fulfilment of both my duty to UCT as well as my duty to this Court . <p> B. COUNCIL MEETING 27 JULY 2024 <p> 19 . On 27 July 2024 , my first full plenary Council meeting , Council was called upon to approve the minutes of the Council meeting of 22 June 2024 , wherein the impugned resolutions had been taken . <p> 20 . In his report , the Interim VC drew attention to challenges the University was experiencing in maintaining relationships with alumni and donors in the wake of the said resolutions , which he referred to in terms of " controversy " . He also said that the University was actively engaging with alumni and donors to ensure that these relationships continued to flourish . <p> 21 . Nonetheless he said that they had still received significant donations recently and listed the R200 million donation from the Donald Gordon Second Foundation ( the " DGF donation " ) in support of the work at the Neurosciences Institute as an example of this . <p> 22 . It has subsequently become evident to me that this depiction underplayed the risk facing the University at the time regarding the DGF donation . In fact , UCT ( through the Interim VC and the Chair ) had already received a letter placing it in breach of this donor agreement on 30 April 2024 , which I first saw when it was attached as Annexure " AA47 " to the Chair 's Answering Affidavit filed on 9 May 2025 . <p> 23 . The letter put UCT on notice to remedy its breach of the DGF contract . Surely , that was a matter pertinent to bring to our attention , especially in light of the fact that Council had to vote on a motion in this meeting on whether to suspend the implementation of the resolutions , pending due diligence assessment ? How can Council members fulfil their fiduciary duties and act responsibly in the face of a risk that they do not know exists ? Neither the Chair nor the Interim VC brought Council members into their confidence . <p> 24 . Only in response to questions later in the meeting about whether the Interim VC was concerned for the University , in light of the ambivalence he had encountered from donors in the wake of the impugned resolutions , did he reply that he believed that it did represent a risk , especially in the case of the donations by DGF and Dell . <p> 25 . He added that the former US Consul General had brought it to his attention that resolutions of this kind could impact grants from federally funded bodies such as the National Institute of Health ( " the NIH " ) . This would have been an opportune moment in the meeting on 27 July 2024 to mention that UCT had been placed on notice of breach by DGF . <p> 26 . The same is true of the Dell Foundation donation . The only mention in this meeting of the Dell donation was in response to the direct question posed to the Interim VC , referred to above . The Interim VC depicted the state of affairs regarding this donation as being at risk . <p> 26.1 . However , on 3 June 2025 , Council members received correspondence from the Dell Foundation relating to the Chair 's answering affidavit in this matter . <p> 26.2 . It revealed that in fact the Dell Foundation had expressed multiple concerns regarding student safety in the context of campus events relating to the Israel ? Gaza conflict since March 2024 , resulting in the Dell Foundation ending its direct funding partnership with UCT on 21 April 2024 . <p> 26.3 . Subsequently , the Dell Foundation further alleges in the letter that they met with the Interim VC in person on 2 May 2024 where they claim the following : " The Foundation strongly reinforced that if UCT 's Council were to pass the motions on or after 22 June 2024 , it would further negatively impact the relationship and ability for the Foundation to reconsider its position and scholarship awards in the future " . <p> 26.4 . If these allegations are true that would render the Interim VC 's depiction of the Dell donation as being ' at risk ' in the 27 July 2024 meeting as inadequate . It would also mean that Council members were ill-equipped to help UCT to navigate its risk management , being blind to the true nature of the risk being faced . <p> 27 . Members of EXCO in May 2023 who were still Council members in July 2024 , also failed to bring the rest of Council into their confidence at the meeting of 27 July 2024 , before voting on the motion to suspend implementation of the resolutions . This became evident when the minutes of the EXCO meeting held on 23 May 2024 were loaded onto Convene in August 2024 . What they reveal is set out below . <p> 27.1 . The Interim VC informed EXCO that Mr Applebaum had written to the Chair and the Interim VC after Senate passed the impugned resolutions , indicating that UCT was in breach of its agreement with the DGF . This related to the clause stipulating that UCT shall adopt a zero tolerance attitude to antisemitism as defined by IHRA . He also suggested a meeting to discuss the types of measures UCT could be taking to remedy the breach . <p> 27.2 . The Interim VC said he had responded to the Applebaums to inform them of the types of measures he had in mind ahead of a meeting . <p> 27.3 . The minutes then record that the Interim VC told EXCO that soon after the Senate resolutions were passed the Dell Foundation had indicated that it was suspending funding to UCT . <p> 27.4 . None of the then EXCO members revealed this in the meeting on 27 July 2024 before our vote was taken . <p> 27.5 . Furthermore , in the context of a contract that UCT had willingly entered into , and before the impugned resolutions were debated in Council on June 22 , I found the response of EXCO members to the above disclosures by the Interim VC surprising . I would have expected the immediate response of EXCO members to this news to entail applying their minds to possible risk mitigation for UCT . However , the minutes of the 23 May 2024 EXCO meeting record that EXCO members " cautioned the University against manipulation by funders that had a pro-Israel stance " , " spoke about donor power and privilege " and " suggested that UCT subscribe to the Jerusalem Declaration " . <p> 27.6 . Concerns regarding non-disclosure stemming from the 23 May EXCO minutes formed the basis of a motion put forward by Professor Moultrie at the 19 October 2024 plenary meeting of Council , resulting in the formation of an Ad Hoe committee into non-disclosure by the Chair , the Interim VC , and members of EXCO in May 2023 . I explore this later in my affidavit . <p> 28 . Later in the 27 July meeting , questions were posed to the interim VC about donors withdrawing or suspending their donations in the aftermath of the resolutions , as well as questions concerning risks to the University regarding American donors possibly being prevented from donating due to American anti-BOS laws . The Interim VC confirmed that no formal assessment had been undertaken on the possible financial consequences of the resolutions for UCT . <p> 28.1 . A member thus put forward a motion that Council needed to conduct an investigation to assure itself that it understood the risk to UCT before implementing the resolutions . He therefore moved that Council pause the implementation of the resolutions pending a due diligence to be designed and conducted by Council . <p> 28.2 . He did not move for the resolutions to be rescinded , as he expressed the view that it would be irrational to rescind the decision of a previous Council based on information that was not yet known . I agreed with this . The paucity of reliable information at this stage made it impossible to exercise a responsible decision either way in my view . <p> 28.3 . A counter-motion was proposed that " while due diligence should be undertaken the implementation of the resolution should not be paused and that the due diligence should take account of the values of the university and move beyond financial issues and the issue of donors " . <p> 28.4 . The counter-motion carried by 16 votes to 1O votes . <p> The Retention of Audio Recordings of Council Meetings <p> 29 . A final pertinent matter to this litigation that first arose at the 27 July meeting of Council is the enduring debate concerning the retention of , and access to , audio recordings of <p> Council meetings . <p> 30 . A newly appointed member of Council requested the audio recording of 22 June 2024 for the purposes of being able to confirm the minutes of that meeting , which had taken place before he was on Council . Council had been newly formed on 1 July 2024 and only 14 of the 30-member Council were returning members who had first-hand knowledge of the meeting . <p> 31 . Faced with this request the then Registrar suggested that Council should adopt an approach of disposing of meeting audio recordings once the minutes had been confirmed . <p> 32 . He further advised that it was important for Council to take a decision in this regard in light of the fact that they can be obtained through a Promotion of Access to Information Act ( " PAIA " ) application . <p> 33 . A robust debate followed . In my view , no compelling governance or logistical argument was ever put forward to recommend the destruction of records by any Council member arguing for it . As far as the latter is concerned , these are electronic records which require little effort to store . From a governance perspective they could assist Council members fulfilling their fiduciary obligations to UCT , they can resolve disputed recollections about the contents of prior decisions taken and enhance the University 's commitment to transparent and accountable governance should they be lawfully requested as a result of legal processes . <p> 34 . Council agreed to seek a legal opinion on meeting audio recordings , to be tabled at the Special meeting of 5 October 2024 . <p> C. BUILD UP TO , ANO SPECIAL COUNCIL MEETING OF 5 OCTOBER 2024 <p> Receipt of proceedings : <p> 35 . I was first made aware of the service of these proceedings on 27 August 2024 by way of an email from the then Registrar ( see email thread in Annexure " KPH2 " ) . As expected , the email made reference to a special meeting being convened to discuss the Notice of Motion . <p> 36 . I have treated this matter with urgency from the outset , cognisant of the serious potential consequences of this application for UCT , and for current members of the Council cited individually in our official capacities . <p> 37 . I presumed my sense of urgency for Council to unpack the ramifications of this application together would be shared by the Chair but I soon felt concerned that this was not the case . <p> 38 . Due to the time limits imposed by the Uniform Rules of Court , I assumed the special meeting would be swiftly arranged and I therefore followed up with the then Registrar at the close of business on 28 August ( See email exchange in Annexure " KPH2 " ) . <p> 39 . By the afternoon of 30 August 2024 , I had still not received notice of the special meeting and again followed up : " UCT can not just operate on its own timeframes outside of legal rules and schedules " . <p> 40 . On 1 September 2024 , Council members received an email from the Chair explaining that a meeting would only be called once a legal opinion on the merits of the matter was in hand , which would not be before 30 September 2024 . I was not convinced by <p> this way of thinking and sent a further email to the then Registrar on 1 September 2024 . <p> 41 . The Chair 's email displayed that he understood that the following court timelines applied : Rule 53 Record to be filed by Council by 13 September 2024 ; Applicant to file supplemented notice of motion and founding affidavit by 30 September 2024 ; notice of opposition by Council ( if any ) due by 21 October 2024 ; and answering affidavit by Council due 2 December 2024 . <p> 42 . Nonetheless at this stage , the Chair acting through the then Registrar , had taken the following decisions : <p> 42.1 . To appoint legal representatives without consideration for any potential conflicting interests that could arise in this review stemming from the fact that UCT , Council , the Chair of Council and individual Council members are all cited in the application ; <p> 42.2 . To select Counsel , seek a legal opinion and inform the content and parameters of the brief to Counsel . This included determining what material was relevant and necessary for Counsel to have sight of while drafting the opinion and an assessment of the anticipated costs of the brief to Counsel ; <p> 42.3 . To remove Council members from any participation in the preparation of the Rule 53 record to be filed by 13 September 2024 . <p> 43 . The above decisions were taken by the Chair- <p> 43.1. without convening a Special Meeting of the Council ; <p> 43.2. without obtaining input from the Council members as to the substantive content of those decisions ; <p> 43.3. without making any of the documentation in respect of those decisions available to the Council ; <p> 43.4. without making any documents mentioned in the Founding Affidavit available to Council . <p> 44 . By delaying the special meeting past 30 September 2024 , the Chair therefore precluded Council members from exercising their fiduciary duties to UCT in critical respects . <p> 45 . There were documents referred to in the Founding Affidavit of which I had had no prior sight and which had also not been placed on Convene . These included the two legal opinions obtained by UCT in 2018 and 2019 and the donor contracts referred to . I could not begin to properly assess the allegations contained in the Applicant 's Founding Affidavit without access to at least these documents . <p> 46 . I was placed in the invidious position of being not only a member of the Council , but also a cited Respondent in legal proceedings , and yet important decisions relating to the litigation were already being made with no opportunity for me to assess their basis or to contribute my input . I had no option but to raise my concerns formally . <p> Letter of 5 September 2024 <p> 47 . I was not alone in this feeling . Three fellow Council members , including the 26th Respondent , joined me in sending a letter on 5 September 2024 to the Chair , copied to <p> the Interim VC . This letter was to express our serious governance concerns with the manner in which the Chair had handled this application up to that point . <p> 48 . I confirm the 26th Respondent 's discussion of this letter in his Explanatory Affidavit at paras 130 - 137 and add to it below . The letter is attached to the 26th Respondent 's Explanatory Affidavit as annexure " KP6 " . <p> 49 . In the letter , we placed on record that after this application was filed , some of the signatories had requested information and various documents arising from this application . These included : <p> 49.1 . The brief to UCT 's legal representatives in the application ; <p> 49.2 . The two legal opinions referred to in paragraph 54 of the Applicant 's founding affidavit ; <p> 49.3 . The donor contract referred to in paragraph 52.5 of the founding affidavit ; and , <p> 49.4 . The contents of the rule 53 record that the Chair intended to file . <p> 50 . The letter further recorded that these documents were not provided to those who requested them . The basis for the refusal was that certain of the documents were deemed by the Chair to be confidential , and in other instances , such as the rule 53 record , that they were yet to be filed . It was further communicated that UCT would be guided by its legal representatives on the content of the record . <p> 51 . Paragraph 531 of the answering affidavit correctly points out that two of the authors of this letter , one of whom being me , were not members of the Council in June 2024 when the decision to adopt the impugned resolutions was taken . <p> 52 . This distinction does not suspend my fiduciary duties as a Council member to properly engage in Council 's participation in this Application , which was initiated during my tenure on the Council . I could not do so without access to relevant information . <p> 53 . The letter served at a special meeting of EXCO convened on 5 September 2024 . <p> Surely it would have been proper to engage with the full membership of Council on such an important matter rather than limiting engagement to eight EXCO members ? <p> 53.1 . According to the rules of EXCO ( see Annexure " KPH3 " ) " EXCO has delegated authority for matters , which plenary Council has delegated to if ' , but my experience since joining Council is that this relationship of delegation is often inverted . <p> 53.2 . There are no minutes of this EXCO meeting on Convene . <p> 54 . We were informed by the then Registrar on 6 September 2024 that the letter had been considered at the special EXCO meeting and that EXCO recommended to the Chair that a special meeting of Council should be convened for 5 October 2024 , the first time in which Council would meet subsequent to the application being served on us . ( Annexure " KPH4 " ) . <p> 55 . This was the only response we received to the serious governance concerns we raised in the letter . I therefore took the decision to brief my own attorneys and instructed them to file a Notice of Intention to Abide , as they did on 6 September 2024 . <p> Events leading to 5 October Meeting <p> 56 . On 9 September 2024 , notice of a special Council meeting scheduled for 5 October 2024 was sent out . <p> 57 . On 13 September 2024 an incomplete Rule 53 Record was filed on behalf of UCT and the Chair . <p> 58 . I became aware of this on 17 September 2024 , when my attorneys received a letter from the Applicant 's attorneys informing all represented parties that the Rule 53 Record on behalf of Council was not filed on time . <p> 59 . Had I not filed my Notice of Intention to Abide , I would not have known that the Rule 53 Record had not been timeously filed because neither the then Registrar nor the Chair had communicated this to the Council . <p> 60 . Together with the 26th Respondent , I relayed our concerns regarding the Rule 53 Record in a letter sent on 18 September 2024 ( see Annexure " KPH5 " ) . <p> 60.1 . We emphasised to the Registrar and Chair that : <p> " Like all of our communications with you to this point , this is done in the spirit of cooperative governance and motivated by our desire and obligation to fulfil our fiduciary obligations to act in the best interests of UCT at all times . We are deeply concerned that Council is in a precarious position and this will not be improved without working together in the best interests of the institution . " <p> 60.2 . We reiterated that : " It is precisely because we sought to prevent Council failing in its obligations to the Court and UCT , that we requested that a special meeting be called sooner . Unfortunately , this request was denied . " <p> 60.3 . We reminded the Chair and then Registrar that : <p> " Members of Council perform a public function with certain obligations imposed on them by the Higher Education Act . This includes the obligation to act in the best interests of the University at all times . It is what the Constitutional Court has described as ' a public litigant ' with ' heightened constitutional obligations ' to the courts . " <p> 60.4 . We therefore requested information as to what steps would be taken to ensure that Council complies wth its duties to the Court under Rule 53 . <p> 61 . The Chair responded to me on 24 September 2024 ( see Annexure " KPH6 " ) . <p> 61.1 . He informed me that he had been advised that Council and the individual members of Council may well have been misjoined , which he indicated is open to debate and discussion . <p> 61.2 . He confirmed that Council EXCO met on 5 September and acting on its advice , he has convened a Special Council meeting for 5 October , the notice of which would be posted later in the week . <p> 61.3 . He reminded me " that there is nothing that precludes at least 5 Council members from petitioning the Chair of Council from calling a Special Council meeting for the purpose specified in the petition . To date no such special request has been received ' . <p> 61.4 . He advised that should Council decide to participate in the matter , it will have until 20 October by which to do so . He suggested this aligned with the plenary Council meeting scheduled for 19 October when " we will , inter alia , discuss and decide our approach to the matter . " <p> 61.5 . He assured me that the matter has " received the attention it deserves . " <p> 62 . My concerns remained , for at least the following reasons , which I reflected in my contributions at the meeting on October 5 : <p> 62.1 . If UCT was of the view that council and its members had been misjoined , that would have been for argument to the court . Moreover , It is not for a litigant to unilaterally decide that they do not have to abide by court rules and timeframes . <p> 62.2 . At this juncture , Council had not complied with the requirement to file a record . <p> The rest of Council had not been informed of this . <p> 62.3 . The vast majority of council members had received little to no communication since this application had been filed . This would have been remedied with a swift special meeting . <p> 62.4 . While I could have garnered the support of other members of council to force a special meeting earlier , in my opinion this would have been an aggressive move , as opposed to appealing to the Chair and the then Registrar to call the meeting , which I did . <p> 62.5 . I also had no way of knowing then that they would persist with delaying the meeting until after court deadlines had passed . Had I known that , I might have made different choices . <p> 62.6 . It seemed to me that these early missteps could have been avoided by following simple principles of transparency and good governance . As council <p> members we all agreed to act in the best interests of UCT . It is regrettable that we were not all equally equipped to do so . I believed at the time that this had put the institution at avoidable and considerable risk . <p> 62.7 . For these reasons , notwithstanding the subsequent votes to retrospectively ratify the Chair 's actions and oppose this litigation , it is certainly not " baseless " to suggest that " the Council was not consulted in relation to this litigation " , as the Chair states in para 555 of his Answering affidavit . <p> Special Meeting 5 October 2024 : <p> 63 . At the meeting of 5 October 2024 it was noted that the Applicant 's attorneys had insisted that Council include an extract of the audio recording of the 22 June Council meeting as part of its Rule 53 record . I confirm the 26th Respondent 's discussion of aspects of the debate that followed in his Explanatory Affidavit at paras 146-149 and add to it below . <p> 64 . Reasons advanced for not sharing the audio recordings centred on concerns that it would chill future debate on controversial topics if it was known that the recording could be made public , as well as broad safety concerns . In the latter regard , a member said that " the Zionists will stop at nothing " and those who had spoken out at the June meeting against Israel would be in danger . <p> 65 . In an attempt to refocus and bring down the heat of the conversation , I pointed out that this line of discussion would not advance us towards guiding the University on the questions before Council . <p> 66 . I continued that while the possibility of retaliation and victimisation needed to be borne in mind , on subjects as contested and controversial as this one the same concerns applied to those on both sides of the debate . <p> 67 . Furthermore , these concerns did not place the University above the law , and as such it was required to comply with its legal obligations . I argued that Council should always err on the side of transparency in conducting litigation . <p> 68 . I requested the Chair that future protocol should entail special meetings of Council being called timeously in order for Council to engage with what was required of it , rather than relying on the Chair to act on its behalf without having first received a mandate from Council . <p> 69 . Given the varying concerns expressed by members during the deliberations , it was determined that the then registrar should seek further legal advice on the topic and defer a decision . <p> 70 . In the event , Council resolved by round robin ( announced on 17 October ) against releasing the audio recordings by 16 votes to 8 votes . <p> 71 . Pursuant to the decision at the meeting of 27 July 2024 to seek a legal opinion on the retention of the audio recordings of meetings , the opinion was put before Council for its consideration . I do not disclose the content of that advice , save to say that it was deemed necessary to supplement the scope of the opinion . <p> 72 . In the course of the discussion on any potential risk associated with retaining audio recordings of meetings , the response centred on risks due to leaks . The then Registrar <p> described the leaks from Council as " endemic " for many years and that it had proven virtually impossible to trace the origin of such leaks . <p> 73 . I expressed the view that the fear of leaks should not hinder Council members from accessing information that was necessary to inform their decision making . <p> 74 . The preoccupation with leaks , at the expense of focusing on the work of the University , has been a recurring theme of my time on Council . It occurs to me that if Council embraced transparent and accountable governance the risks associated with leaks would be significantly diminished . <p> 75 . Council resolved to consider the supplementary legal opinion sought at the ordinary meeting on 19 October 2024 . <p> D. MEETING OF 19 OCTOBER 2024 AND RELATED EVENTS <p> 76 . I confirm paras 168 - 177 of the 26th Respondent 's Explanatory Affidavit , which relate to the motions to oppose this application and related motions concerning this case , passed at the meeting of 19 October 2024 , and add the following : <p> 76.1 . I too voted against each of the motions . <p> 76.2 . I raised a concern around the question of a potential conflict of interest in having one set of legal representatives representing UCT , the Council itself and the individual council members simultaneously . <p> Ad Hoe Committee on Non-Disclosure : <p> 77 . I was present throughout the Council meeting on 19 October 2024 and I am a member of the Ad Hoe Committee which was formed as a result of the morning 's discussions . <p> 78 . On 19 October Council was called upon to approve the minutes of the 27 July 2024 Council meeting . <p> 79 . In between the Council meetings of July and October , the 23 May 2024 EXCO minutes were loaded onto Convene . <p> 80 . This is relevant because a review of the May EXCO minutes appeared to contradict what was reported by the Interim VC at the July meeting , which could have influenced the Council 's votes in both June and July 2024 . <p> 81 . Professor Moultrie raised a concern that , in light of the EXCO minutes , the potential misinformation to Council by the Interim VC may have misled the Council . <p> 82 . Reference was made to the Interim VC 's discussion of the R200 million grant from the DGF and the risks associated with donor ambivalence , reflected in the minutes of 27 July . <p> 83 . I quote the relevant portion of the 19 October minutes here in full as Professor Moultrie 's comments encapsulate the circumstances behind the formation of the Ad Hoe Committee : <p> " Prof Moultrie noted that while the minutes were accurate , a significant issue arose from the fact that the Council only received the minutes from the May 2024 EXCO meeting in August . In that May 2024 meeting , the interim Vice-Chancellor indicated that Hilton Applebaum had informed them that UCT was in breach of <p> an agreement , and that the Dell Foundation would be suspending funding . He emphasized that this information raised critical questions , especially since eight members of the current Council were present at the May 2024 Exco meeting . <p> He pointed out that seven members were present at the 22 June 2024 meeting ... Five of those members remained on the Council , and the interim Vice ? Chancellor was in office during both meetings . However , there was no evidence that anyone from the EXCO informed the Council about the critical information discussed in the May 2024 EXCO meeting . As a result , the Council made decisions without being aware of significant issues , such as the R 200 million donation that UCT had been put on notice about . He argued that the interim Vice ? Chancellor was not truthful about the risks associated with the Dell Foundation and the DGF , framing them as mere risks when they were actual material risks . The Council proceeded to vote without the necessary context , and those Council members who attended the May 2024 meeting did not correct the interim Vice ? Chancellor 's misleading statements . Consequently , other Council members were unaware of these facts when they were revealed in August 2024 . He emphasized that regardless of the merits of the motions discussed , there was a significant governance crisis due to the failure to disclose crucial information about a R 200 million donation . This amount could fund 1O professors or 50 PhD students per year . He questioned the rationality of the Council 's decisions , suggesting that had they been aware of this substantial donation being withdrawn , their votes may have differed . He expressed concern about a compromise to the integrity of the decisions made . <p> Professor Moultrie argued that Council members who were not part of the EXCO , either in the current or previous council , were unaware of the important accusations from the May 2024 EXCO meeting . This information should have been presented to the Council before their decision on 22 June 2024 in respect of the motions on the Gaza/Israeli conflict and during the discussions of motions on 27 July 2024 . He asserted that the faiiure to disclose this material information constitutes a governance failure by the interim Vice-Chancellor and the other EXCO members who attended those meetings , as they did not adequately inform the Council . " <p> 84 . I confirm that I was not aware that DGF had notified the University of a breach of their donation contract , nor that the Dell Foundation had terminated its relationship with UCT when I voted on the motion to suspend the Resolutions in July 2024 ( cf para 573 of the Answering Affidavit ) . <p> 85 . Members of Council criticised donors and deferred responsibility , as captured in the following extract from the minutes : <p> " The member reminded Council that she was involved in formulating a Council resolution based on informed principles . She did not believe that there were any untruths from the interim Vice-Chancellor . She emphasized that all Council members understood the risks associated with the R200 million donation to the Neuroscience Institute . The resolution was created in the context of national higher education legislation that outlines governance responsibilities , <p> including public accountability , social responsibility , institutional autonomy , and academic freedom . She argued that the resolution was necessary to prevent the University from being pressured by wealthy donors to compromise its core values . She highlighted ethical concerns regarding the DGF , which insisted on naming rights in exchange for funding , raising implications for tax regulations . She noted that this was not the first instance of the DGF withdrawing funds . She criticized the DGF for using strong-arm tactics to influence the University when their views were not aligned with the institution 's approach . She expressed concern about the ethical implications of accepting funding from donors who may have questionable backgrounds , comparing it to potentially accepting funds from organized crime . She questioned the morality of such decisions , especially in light of serious issues like genocide currently being addressed at the International Court of Justice " . <p> 86 . I found this to be extraordinary in the circumstances . No substantiation had been provided for any of the claims being made . They were stated as conclusions without any evidence . My reply is captured in the minutes as follows : <p> " Ms Phelps expressed confusion regarding the previous discussion , particularly about how the Council could make informed decisions when many members were not aware of critical information . She noted that this situation represented a governance crisis that required serious attention . She argued against labelling a donor 's actions as bullying , stating that the University had the right to accept or reject donation terms . She asserted that all members of the EXCO were aware of the terms and the breach , which was not disclosed to the Council . She concluded that ethical considerations should not compromise governance standards , which must be upheld regardless of the topic under discussion , highlighting a clear failure in governance practices . She urged Council members to exercise caution regarding defamation , emphasizing that unsupported allegations , such as labelling individuals as war criminals or involved in organized crime , must be backed by evidence " . <p> 87 . It should be noted that at the most recent Council meeting held on 28 June 2025 the current VC 's report included a report on the advice from the law firm ENS , given to UCT in July 2024 . UCT was advised that there are no tax implications arising from the insistence of naming rights by a donor such as DGF , as the member quoted above suggested . <p> 88 . EXCO Members speaking against Professor Moultrie highlighted the " complexity and uncertainty " surrounding the ongoing engagements with the donors . <p> 88.1 . There was no uncertainty about UCT 's being in breach . <p> 88.2 . It has come to light that on 26 April 2024 UCT had received the breach letter from the DGF and on 21 April 2024 UCT had been notified by Dell of their decision to terminate their direct funding partnership with the institution . <p> 88.3 . Yet the Interim VC and members of EXCO withheld this information from Council twice when voting on the resolutions - on 22 June and again on 27 July . <p> 89 . To move the meeting forward , the Chair proposed that the Council should appoint an ad hoe subcommittee to investigate the issues raised by Professor Moultrie and noted that Council members were now aware of the issues related to the DGF and the Dell Foundation : " The chair agreed that the issue was serious and repeated his proposal that a subcommittee should be appointed to investigate the matter and report back to councif ' . <p> 90 . Council resolved to appoint 7 Council members to the sub-committee , one of whom being me . By the time the committee first met , one of the members was no longer on Council and thus the committee continued with the remaining 6 members . <p> 91 . Council also agreed that Professor Moultrie should draft the initial terms of reference , which should then be circulated to Council members for comment . The comments would be taken into account and be circulated to Council members for a decision by round robin . <p> 92 . I confirm paras 178 -184 of the 26th Respondent 's Explanatory Affidavit dealing with the Ad Hoe Committee 's Terms of Reference and add to this below . <p> 93 . On 6 November 2024 , myself and another member of the subcommittee addressed a letter to the Registrar 's Secretariat and the current VC expressing our serious concerns related to the formulation of the terms of reference for the ad hoe committee ( see Annexure " KPH7 " ) . Our concerns specifically addressed the following : <p> 93.1. whether conflicted Council members had contributed to the consolidated terms of reference ; <p> 93.2. the contents of the consolidated terms of reference ; <p> 93.3. the proposed punitive sanctions against Professor Moultrie ; and <p> 93.4. the Chair 's note to the Council sent on 4 November 2024 detailing a process for concluding the ad hoe committee 's terms of reference that did not align with our understanding of Council 's deliberations . <p> 94 . With regard to how the process to formulate the terms of reference concluded I confirm paras 190 - 192 of the Explanatory Affidavit of the 26th Respondent . <p> 95 . The Ad Hoe Committee convened on the following dates : 19 November 2024 and 14 March 2025 . <p> 96 . At the 19 November meeting , the committee considered the initial terms of reference alongside the consolidated terms of reference in order to reach a final version of these to put before Council . <p> 97 . These served before Council on 7 December 2024 and were adopted . <p> 98 . At the 14 March 2025 meeting of the ad hoe committee , the committee developed a work plan to give effect to the terms of reference . It also determined the documents required for the investigation , identified the witnesses to be interviewed and agreed on time frames for the process . Meeting notes were circulated after the meeting concluded for the purpose of the Chair of the ad hoe committee providing an update to Council in its plenary meeting the next day . <p> 99 . The Council meeting on 15 March ran over time and the Chair of the ad hoe committee had to leave before the update had been given to Council . On his way out he thus asked me to present the meeting notes from 14 March . <p> 100 . I expected the update to be uncomplicated , as it was merely a matter for noting on the Council agenda . What transpired was a sustained attempt by the Chair and other members of Council , some of whom were conflicted , to frustrate the subcommittee fulfilling its functions . <p> 100.1 . Concerns raised centred on : confidentiality in light of the risks of leaks and ; legal privilege arising from the fact that this application was before the court . <p> 100.2 . Regarding the risk of leaks , I reassured Council that the risk was significantly reduced by the small size of the committee , consisting of only 6 members . This would make it very easy to identify a leak and should act as a disincentive for anyone considering leaking information . <p> 100.3 . Regarding legal privilege , I reiterated that this attaches to the whole of Council and therefore was no impediment to the work of the committee proceeding in the face of this litigation . <p> 100.4 . Nonetheless , the Chair and other conflicted Council members persisted with calling for the work of the committee to be halted , despite no procedural or substantive grounds for doing so . <p> 100.5 . I emphasised the importance of not revisiting recent decisions of Council when these decisions had been taken in a procedurally proper way , as was the case with the ad hoe committee . <p> 100.6 . Council noted my report and concluded the debate . <p> 100.7 . In the course of the debate certain council members had revealed a greater preoccupation with preventing leaks than performing the critical self ? governance functions of Council . <p> 101 . In the event , the work of the subcommittee has been eclipsed by subsequent developments on Council , including a number of Code of Conduct violation complaints against Council members . <p> 102 . I therefore suggested to the Chair of the ad hoe committee that we propose to Council that the ad hoe committee be disbanded and absorbed into the complaints process . <p> 103 . In so doing , I cited the Chair 's contentions in para 579 of the Answering Affidavit , that all members of the committee are potentially conflicted , whereas the complaints process before Council will be adjudicated by a neutral third party . <p> 104 . The ad hoe committee resolved to follow my suggestion , and the proposal served before Council at its meeting on 28 June 2025 . We did not complete the agenda for the 28 <p> June meeting , thus the matter remains undecided . <p> Audio recordings of Council meetings : <p> 105 . The matter of the retention or destruction of Council audio recordings and the legal opinions sought on this topic arose for discussion again at the meeting of 19 October . I will not divulge the content of the legal advice received . <p> 106 . It was noted that Council needed to develop a policy in this regard and a debate ensued on the merits or risks of retaining such recordings . <p> 107 . In the course of the debate the chair noted that there were benefits to retaining the recordings but these must be weighed with the risks , and suggested retaining the recordings for a limited period and then destroying them . In the course of this submission he noted as a risk the following , as captured in the minutes : <p> " He noted under the Promotion of Access to Information Act ( PA/A ) , there may be a requirement to disclose such recordings , particularly in the <p> context of legal requests like the one made during the Mendelsohn case ... The Chair expressed concern that future litigation could arise , potentially forcing the Council and its successors into a difficult position regarding the availability of these recordings " . <p> 108 . My response to the Chair is also captured in the minutes , as follows : <p> " She cautioned that destroying the recordings could place the Council in a precarious position , especially since there seems to be no logistical or financial burden associated with keeping them . She raised the issue of PA/A applications , arguing that if a legally valid request was made and granted by the courts , it would be improper for the University to resist compliance ... She concluded that the University had a public duty to uphold the rule of law by cooperating with legitimate requests , reinforcing the importance of transparency and accountability in its operations . " <p> 109 . This elicited a response from the Chair that I found surprisingly aggressive . He insinuated that I was naive in maintaining a stance that , simply because the law required something , it was always necessary to abide by it . To illustrate this , he used an analogy of the Apartheid government issuing a State of Emergency and asked me whether simply because this had happened it meant that lawyers should acquiesce . <p> 109.1 . I asked that my objection to this analogy , and the tone in which it was given , be entered onto the record of the meeting . <p> 109.2 . I stated there is an obvious distinction between the idea of complying with a lawful PAIA application and complying with the illegitimate Apartheid government 's declaration of a state of emergency . <p> 109.3 . The analogy was clearly designed to condescend and therefore delegitimise my earlier input on the need for transparency and accountability as necessary constituents of good governance for public institutions . <p> 11O . The proposed policy on the retention of Council audio recordings is currently sitting before the Governance Committee , who are yet to present it to Council for approval . <p> VC Report 19 October Meeting <p> 111 . At the meeting on 19 October the current VC gave a detailed and substantive report to Council . It included a frank discussion assessing the impact of implementing the impugned resolutions on the University 's financial support from donors . <p> 112 . He focused on reporting current figures rather than forecasting potential future funding losses . He highlighted the withdrawals of DGF and Dell , estimating the total funding losses at around R250 million , with concerns about future funding losses not yet fully assessed . <p> 113 . His report also addressed the " polarization that was happening in relation to the Gaza resolution " . The Vice-Chancellor highlighted the discomfort among donors primarily related to the second resolution , which was perceived by some donors to interfere with academic freedom . <p> 114 . The Vice-Chancellor warned that failing to address concerns about academic freedom could jeopardize UCT 's academic integrity and reputation . He emphasized the need for measures to protect the University 's standing and sought the Council 's support in this effort . <p> 115 . A member responded to the report by highlighting the significance of a potential financial loss of R250 million rand . He provided context by noting that the current financial deficit for the year was approximately R350 million , which did not include this potential loss . This indicated that the financial situation could worsen further . <p> 116 . The member emphasized the importance of addressing the immediate financial loss of a significant amount of money rather than speculating about potential future funding sources . <p> 117 . Nonetheless , certain Council members have continued to under-play the financial impact of the resolutions for UCT , as was evident in the Council meeting on 15 March , discussed below . <p> 118 . The debates pertaining to the impugned resolutions and this application , in the meeting of 19 October , were tense , as they have tended to be whenever this topic has come up in meetings . My experience has been that anyone construed as speaking against the retention of the resolutions is either silenced , dismissed or accused of being part of either <p> a ' far-right ' , ' zionist ' or ' pro-zionist ' plot . <p> 119 . I made a contribution in the meeting calling for Council members not to put their personal views on the conflict in the Middle East above their focus on rational decision-making in the best interests of UCT , when determining our response to the founding affidavit . <p> 120 . In noting my contribution the chair observed that I had made my " strong views known on the subject " of the impugned resolutions , despite the fact that all of my contributions had centered around UCT 's duties and interests and not the subject of the resolutions themselves . <p> 121 . To allay the impression that I had any conflicted interests in the performance of my duties as a Council member , I immediately sent the chair an email and tackled the issue head-on ( see Annexure " KPH8 " ) . <p> 122 . Despite the Chair responding positively and affirming my role on Council in the email exchange , I have continued to experience hostility and conspiracy theories about right ? wing and/ or zionist plots on Council in the performance of my functions . <p> E. EVENTS OF 2025 <p> 123 . On 26 January 2025 at 18h45 members of Council received a Council Circular from the Chair reminding us of the Council resolution to oppose this application and advising that the Chair was due to meet with the legal team the following day to prepare an answering affidavit . Council members were invited to submit any input by 14h00 on 27 January 2025 ( see Annexure " KPH9 " ) . <p> 124 . I replied to this email on 27 January 2025 ( see Annexure " KPH10 " ) expressing the following concerns : <p> " I note the following with regards to the Council Circular ( 26 January 2025 ) : <p> 1 . Providing less than 24 hours ' notice to provide the feedback requested is unfeasible and inappropriate . <p> 2 . We have not received any updated guidance from the legal team on prospects of success since receiving the Mendelsohn Amended Notice of Motion . Yet it appears the Chair and legal team are proceeding with the earlier decision to oppose the application . <p> 3 . I did not vote in favour of opposing the Application on 19 October 2024 , which vote , in my opinion , was premature . <p> 4 . In light of the Amended Notice of Motion , it also strikes me there are conflicts of interest operating now , which have not been explored with Council . <p> 5 . The reference to ' factual aspects arising from the Supplementary Founding Affidavit ' is vague at best . <p> 5.1 As you know I am a member of the Ad Hoe Committee formed to investigate the facts surrounding the withdrawal of funding by the Dell and Donald Gordon Foundations . The committee has not yet commenced its work . If this falls under the ' factual aspects ' referred to above , then it is precipitous and unsound to make any reflections in this regard . We are simply not possessed with the knowledge . <p> 5.2 Regarding the Chair 's answering affidavit - only he is in possession of the facts pertaining to his affidavit . It is not possible for me or anyone else on Council to comment on facts that are only in his possession . " <p> 125 . To the best of my knowledge I received no answer to the concerns raised above . <p> 126 . In contrast to the short shrift given to gathering the input of the full membership of Council before filing the Answering Affidavit , the Chair avers at para 701 of the Answering Affidavit that he consulted extensively and at length with the University 's legal team and obtained input on the answering affidavit from numerous other persons , including persons within the University but not on Council . <p> 127 . The Chair avers at para 701 of the Answering Affidavit that the legal team consulted with the EXCO members who attended both the 23 May 2025 Exec meeting and the 22 June 2024 meeting of Council . <p> 128 . The Chair avers at para 702.2 of the Answering Affidavit that the input and sign off of several people , including senior office holders in the University was required to ensure accuracy . <p> 129 . The Chair makes reference at para 705.2 of the Answering Affidavit to the need for the University 's legal team to consult further with persons who were present at the 15 March 2025 meeting ( at para 705.2 ) . <p> 130 . I was present at this Council meeting . I was not consulted for input . The choice of which Council members were consulted in preparing the Answering Affidavit on behalf of Council , and therefore which views are reflected in it , has been selective . <p> 131 . I first saw the Answering Affidavit on behalf of Council on 9 May 2025 , having received it through my attorneys . It was released to all other members of Council on 11 May 2025 on Convene . <p> 132 . As illustrated throughout this affidavit , the Answering Affidavit contained information and documents of which I was previously unaware and statements that are not aligned with the facts . That led me to file this affidavit . <p> 133 . The 26th Respondent concurred with my reaction to the answering affidavit . He and I wrote to the Registrar to convey our concern that certain of the facts alleged by the Chair in his answering affidavit on behalf of Council are inconsistent with our recollection of what was said in Council meetings . This correspondence is dealt with in paras 11.1 above . <p> 134 . By way of context , the business of Council in 2025 has been chaotic , characterised by numerous meetings , unfinished agendas and an emphasis on Council leaks and complaints . It is my view that this is a result of more emphasis being placed on protecting the interests of certain Council members in light of this litigation than on the best interests of UCT . The following meetings refer : <p> 134.1 . The ordinary meeting of Council held on 15 March 2025 . The agenda could not be completed in the allocated time . <p> 134.2 . The reconvened ordinary Council meeting on 12 April 2025 , having originally been scheduled for 29 March 2025 but then twice postponed , first to 5 April 2025 and then to 12 April 2025 . The agenda could not be completed in the allocated time . <p> 134.3 . The reconvened ordinary Council meeting on 1O May 2025 , which lost quorum . <p> 134.4 . The special Council meeting on 19 May 2025 . <p> 134.5 . The special Council meeting on 12 June 2025 . This meeting was adjourned until 19 June 2025 , which meeting was ultimately cancelled . <p> 134.6 . The ordinary meeting of Council on 28 June 2025 . The agenda could not be completed in the allocated time . A special meeting is yet to be called to conclude the agenda . <p> 134.7 . The section 58 ( Institutional Statute ) meeting of Council , held after the ordinary Council meeting on 28 June 2025 . <p> 135 . This chaos has impaired Council 's ability to timeously attend to the work of the institution . As a result , another member of Council and I implored the Council at the 15 March meeting to place the work of the University at the front of the agenda and deal with the controversial matters at the end of the meeting . Our pleas were not heeded . <p> Council Meeting 15 March 2025 : <p> 136 . Much of this dysfunction on Council in 2025 stems from the meeting of 15 March , described by the 26th Respondent in paras 198 - 208 , which I confirm and add only what follows below . <p> 137 . I was present at this meeting when the events referred to by the Chair in his Answering affidavit at paras 294 and 295 occurred . <p> 138 . With reference to para 294 of the Answering Affidavit , it is not true to suggest that the current VC 's verbal report aligned in every way with his written report . It differed in a material aspect . That is , in his written report he only referred to modifying the impugned resolutions . In his verbal report he issued an impassioned plea to Council to support the efforts of the executive to navigate a looming financial crisis , which he and the DVC for Research referred to as ' existential ' , and to consider rescinding the current resolutions . <p> 138.1 . The VC stated that rescinding the resolutions would enable him to mitigate the situation caused by the loss of NIH funding . <p> 138.2 . The VC indicated that Council was asking the executive to work with its hands tied behind its back while the current resolutions remained in place , as they were inhibiting fundraising . <p> 138.3 . The VC confirmed that donor concerns around the current resolutions did not all pertain to antisemitism but included various concerns relating to limiting academic freedom . <p> 139 . With reference to para 295 of the Answering Affidavit , the Chair refers to the VC in the following terms : " ... the VC did not say that the rescission or amendment of the Gaza Resolutions would be a ' silver bullet ' that would neutralise the risk of losing US federal funding " . This is a misrepresentation . <p> 139.1 . The words ' silver bullet ' were in fact first said by the Chair . He initiated a heated exchange with the VC when he used the phrase to mis-characterise what the VC had actually said with regard to the impugned resolutions , and their negative impact on the Executive 's struggles to secure donor funding . <p> 139.2 . In response , the VC suggested that the Chair had put words in his mouth , and sensationalised his remarks . The VC further pointed out that he had never claimed that rescinding the resolutions would be a silver bullet , but rather that they were significantly hampering the Executive 's ability to fundraise . <p> 139.3 . This exchange has been placed out of context in para 295 of the Answering Affidavit , thereby distorting the true events before the Council . <p> Special meeting of 12 June 2025 : <p> 140 . Between March and June 2025 the DGF and Dell Foundation respectively sent correspondence for Council 's attention in response to their reading the Chair 's Answering Affidavit and the accompanying confirmatory and supporting affidavits thereto . They expressed concern with the factual veracity of various allegations made in the affidavits . <p> 141 . In circulating the DGF letter , the Chair mentioned legal advice that he had received to the effect that there was no legal obligation for him to make the DGF May 22 letter available to us as Council members . <p> 141.1 . Neither the brief requesting the legal advice , nor the legal advice he referred to , was disclosed to Council members . Nor was it clear on whose authority the advice was sought . <p> 141.2 . It is difficult to understand how a letter to Council that is addressed to all Council members can be legally withheld from the Council . <p> 141.3 . Nonetheless , the Chair indicated that he was making it " available in the interests of transparency . " <p> 142 . The lack of candour that the Chair and others have shown with Council regarding the true state of affairs with the DGF and Dell donations has not only frustrated Council members from performing their fiduciary duties , but has compromised the reputation of the University in the eyes of donors , as the two letters display . For example , the DGF expressed that " Never in our 54-year history have we experienced vilification such as that embodied in Mr Arendse 's affidavit . " <p> 143 . Extremely serious contentions from the perspective of the fiduciary duties of Council members were made by the donors , including inter-alia that " Mr Arendse 's allegations are false ... Mr Arendse knows these allegations to be false . " <p> 144 . By this stage it was apparent that Council was facing a governance crisis and urgent intervention was required to try and exercise our duty of self-governance . <p> 145 . Myself and four other concerned members of Council therefore exercised the provisions of s19(1) ( b ) of the Institutional Statute to convene a Special Meeting of the <p> Council as soon as reasonably possible . We believed we would be failing in our fiduciary duties to UCT if we stood by and witnessed this governance crisis without trying to intervene . <p> 146 . One of the possible outcomes of the special meeting we called made allowance for passing a motion of no confidence in certain members of Council . Although the passing of a motion of no confidence has no enforceable ramifications , we hoped it could provide an important conduit for holding Council members accountable and placing them in a position where they could choose to act accountably . A copy of this request for a special meeting of 2 June 2025 is attached as Annexure " KPH11 " . <p> 147 . The Dell letter arrived subsequent to our calling the special meeting , on 3 June 2025 . <p> Like the DGF letter , the Dell Foundation also contends that the affidavit deposed to by the Chair is incorrect in several respects . This only emphasised the need for the special meeting we had called . <p> 148 . The meeting was convened for 12 June 2025 . <p> 149 . The special meeting was extraordinary . The Chair arrived late and immediately launched into an ad hominem attack of the Interim Registrar for allowing the meeting to happen . His voice was raised , his irritation was unmasked and that behaviour continued for approximately three hours , being directed variably to the Registrar and any other proposers of the special meeting who tried to speak , or Council members who tried to intervene to stop the Chair abusing his position . <p> 150 . The Chair refused to relinquish his chair for this meeting , despite being conflicted . <p> 151 . Anyone he perceived would support the call for a special meeting was silenced . This included somebody manually muting the 26th Respondent 's microphone on Microsoft Teams repeatedly while he was trying to introduce the call for a special meeting . My hand was left raised for over two hours despite my noting on multiple occasions how long I had been waiting to speak . At the instance of the Chair , the floor was only given to Council members objecting to the special meeting . <p> 152 . The meeting was disrupted by a group of Council members working in concert to stop the proposers of the special meeting from being able to proceed . The atmosphere was threatening towards us , designed to intimidate us and replete with baseless accusations , gaslighting and impugning our motivations for calling the special meeting , without letting us speak to why the meeting had been called . <p> 153 . For example , I recall one of the Council members who was a subject of the call for a special meeting referred to the motion as " a particular political agenda " , likening it to events in the USA impacting on the autonomy of American universities . She then called it " a Zionist agenda " and referred to the state of Israel , " which is a genocidal state , which is killing children " . She concluded that " we are being held ransom by the Zionists on our council . " <p> 154 . Having successfully obstructed the special meeting for over three hours it was then resolved that the special meeting be adjourned to 19 June 2025 . The Interim Registrar was requested to obtain another legal opinion on certain procedural aspects that had been fought over in this meeting . <p> 155 . In the event , the legal opinion came in on the evening of 18 June 2025 . It did not align with previous legal advice that Council had received . This prompted me to send an <p> email on behalf of the callers of the special meeting to withdraw our meeting request ( see Annexure " KP13 " to the Explanatory Affidavit of the 26th Respondent ) . <p> We emphasised that : <p> " we do not wish to act in the face of legal uncertainty . We believe that UCT 's best interests would not be served by acting in the face of legal uncertainty and persisting with a special meeting under these circumstances . <p> We therefore withdraw our call for a special meeting . <p> Nonetheless , we have a duty to state that the conduct of council members in the special meeting last week and resulting correspondence , including ad hominem accusations , have only served to amplify our governance concerns , rather than ameliorate them . " <p> F. CONCLUSION <p> 156 . I have addressed the complexity of the moral issues at the heart of the impugned resolutions in previous Council meetings , emphasizing that Council members must prioritise the best interests of the university in all decision-making . Even in contentious moral debates , such as the one at hand , the duty to put the best interests of the university first is not suspended . <p> 157 . I have argued in meetings that safeguarding the institution 's future is a duty , and downplaying the harsh realities faced by the university is dangerous and reckless . The sustainability of the University should not be sidelined on the altar of divisive debates . <p> 158 . As Council members , sometimes difficult decisions must be made , even where they may garner criticism . This is why we have objective principles of good governance to guide us . <p> 159 . Supporting the impugned resolutions and exercising fiduciary duties to mitigate risks for UCT should not be mutually exclusive , but in my experience on Council as it relates to this application , they have too often been approached this way . <p> 160 . Moral considerations are nuanced and can be difficult to navigate , especially in a large body like UCT Council , comprising of a cross-section of different perspectives . The diversity of these perspectives has not been given purchase in the course of Council 's engagement with this Court . <p> 161 . We have an excellent new executive installed at UCT but we are failing in our primary job to support them to steer the university through turbulent times with accountable and transparent governance . My deposing to this affidavit is both in fulfilment of my duty of candour to this Court , as well as my commitment to act in the best interests of UCT . <p> 162 . I have set out these perspectives to the Court to demonstrate the nature of the strong dissensus within the Council regarding the resolutions and this application .
