Columbia ( and many other universities ) are objectively unsafe for Jewish students , irrespective of their stance on Israel or the definition of antisemitism adopted by them . <p> 31 . By way of example , I annex hereto as " WK2 " , a Class Action Complaint issued out of the United States District Court , Southern District of New York , by one Carrie Goldbern ( ' Ms Goldbern ' ) , on behalf of all other similarly situated persons against the Trustees of Columbia University . <p> 32 . It is evident from complaint that Ms Goldbern recognises the l ? gitimacy of debates and disputes regarding the Israel-Palestine issue . However , she refers to those protestors who have ' gone well beyond simply engaging in free speech and who have different and menacing goals . This extreme element is not just expressing dissent ; they have and are continuing to commit acts of violence , they are intimidating and harassing Jewish students and faculty members , they are inciting demonstrators to engage in hate speech and also commit acts of violence , which has been taking place , and they have even called for tefforist attacks against the United States and the State of Israel ' . <p> 33 . Ms. Goldbern refers to the active blockading and harassment of Jewish students , who are targeted with statements such as ' Death to Jews ' , ' Long life Hamas ' , ' there is only one solution , Intifada revolution ' and ' go back to Poland ' . She refers to students who have outward manifestations of their Judaism such as wearing a.Star of David necklace , being targeted and attacked , and facing threats such as ' the 7th of October is going to be every day for you ' . <p> 34 . Similar class action complaints have been lodged by students at Harvard University ( ' Harvard ' ) and New York University ( ' NYU ' ) , raising similar examples of antisemitism faced by students on campus . <p> 34.1 . I attach the Harvard complaint hereto as " WK3 " . <p> 34.2 . I attach the NYU complaint hereto as " WK4 " . <p> 35 . There can be no doubt that antisemitism pervades campuses in the United States , irrespective of the content of the definitions of antisemitism adopted . By way of example : <p> 35.1 . In November 2023 , Columbia created the Task Force on Antisemitism ( ' the Colombia Taskforce ' ) to address the harmful impact of antisemitism on members of Columbia 's Jewish community and to ensure that protection , respect , and belonging extends to everyone , which has produced three reports . The Colombia Taskforce Report #2 ( ' Columbia Report #2 ' ) entitled ' Columbia University Student Experiences of Antisemitism and Recommendations for Promoting Shared Values and Inclusion ' documents Colombia student experiences of antisemitism across a variety of university activities and platforms . <p> 35.2 . The Colombia Report #2 drew on the numerous accounts shared with the Colombia Taskforce over several months to produce its own working definition of antisemitism , instead of relying on any existing definitions in order to craft a working definition rooted in recent experiences at Columbia . The Columbia Report #2 developed the following working definition of antisemitism : <p> ' Antisemitism is prejudice , discrimination , hate , or violence directed at Jews , including Jewish Israelis . Antisemitism can manifest in a range of ways , including as ethnic slurs , epithets , and caricatures ; stereotypes ; antisemitic tropes and symbols ; Holocaust denial ; targeting Jews or Israelis for violence or celebrating violence against them ; exclusion or discrimination based on Jewish identity or ancestry or real or perceived ties to Israel ; and certain double standards applied to Israel ' . <p> 35.3 . Using this definition , the Columbia Report #2 records a litany of antisemitic experiences of Jewish and non-Jewish students including : <p> 35.3.1 . One student who had moved into her dorm room in September 2024 , placed a mezuzah on her doorway as required by ritual law , as traditional Jews have done for centuries . In October 2024 , people began banging on her door at all hours of the night , that demanding she explain Israel 's actions . She was forced to move out of the dorm . <p> 35.3.2 . Students reported having necklaces ripped off their necks and being pinned against walls , while walking back to their dorms on Friday afternoon and when they were on their way to synagogue . <p> 35.3.3 . On 23 January 2024 , the Law School Student Senate voted against the official recognition of a proposed student group called Law Students Against Antisemitism . According to its constitution , the group planned on organising educational events , encouraging dialogue about antisemitism at the Law School , and providing resources and opportunities for students both to learn about antisemitism and find support , should they be targeted by antisemitic acts . Of the nine student groups and clubs that requested approval that year , Law Students Against Antisemitism was the only one that was denied . <p> 35.3.4 . Non-Jewish , Israeli , or Zionist students who tried to maintain a neutral stance were shut down or bullied into silence . A student from Sewa , a Sikh student group at Columbia , reported that she was ousted from her role as co-president because of her supposed support for Israel , when she had actually tried to maintain a neutral position . Speaking as a guest on the student-led podcast , she reported that her attempts to keep the group politically neutral at a time when several members wished to publicly support Columbia University Apartheid Divest ( ' CUAD ' ) , led to backlash from her peers . Other members of the group did not support CUAD 's demands , but ultimately , she said , she was pressured to step down from her position . <p> 35.4 . A copy of Report #2 is annexed hereto as " WK5 " . <p> 35.5 . In April 2025 Harvard released the Final Report of the Presidential Task Force on Combating Antisemitism and Anti-Israeli Bias ( ' the Harvard Report ' ) , a copy of which is annexed hereto as " WK6 " . The Harvard Presidential Task Force on Combating Antisemitism and Anti-Israeli Bias ( ' the Harvard Task Force ' ) was established in January 2024 to examine the recent history of antisemitism and its current manifestations on the Harvard campus with the aim of identifying causes of and contributing factors to anti-Jewish behaviours on the Harvard campus ; evaluate evidence regarding the characteristics and frequency of these behaviours ; and recommend approaches to combat antisemitism and its impact on the Harvard campus . <p> 35.6 . The Harvard Report starts with an account of a Jewish student in respect of a forum in which student fellowship recipients share their stories in short speeches . <p> 35.6.1 . The Jewish student had planned to describe how their experiences as a grandchild of Holocaust survivors inspired their career goals . In prepared remarks shared with the forum 's student organisers , the student described how their grandfather survived the Holocaust by migrating to the then-British Mandate of Palestine , and ultimately helped tens of thousands of others find refuge in territory that is now part of Israel . The student reported that ' the student directors of the conference pulled me aside and said that I can not mention my grandfather 's rescue missions in my speech , because his rescue missions involve Israel . Nowhere does my speech mention the current war or Zionism . It is strictly about the Holocaust . The two student organizers told me that my family 's Holocaust narrative is not " tasteful " and ... I asked " what is not tasteful ? " One of the students laughed in my face and said , " oh my God . " This response was incredibly hurtful and inappropriate . They told me that my family history is inherently one-sided because it does not acknowledge the displacements of Palestinian populations ... ' , According to the student speaker , while the forum organisers eventually allowed the speaker to mention their grandfather 's rescue mission , they insisted that the speaker omit reference to the British Mandate of Palestine as their grandfather 's destination . <p> 35.7 . The Harvard Report found that Jewish Harvard affiliates did not think in terms of clear , formal definitions of antisemitism , but rather were likely to complain about antisemitism when they felt that people were treating them differently because they were Jewish . For example , when other students ( Jewish or not ) told them that what they were experiencing was not antisemitism , Jewish students often felt this negation of their actual experiences and perceptions as a form of bias that was inconsistent with the prevailing anti-bias framework that prioritises ' lived experiences ' and ' discriminatory impact ' . <p> 35.8 . In preparing the Harvard Report the authors recognised that no one definition of antisemitism can be applied to all contexts , and consequently the Harvard the Task Force had opted to prioritize faculty , staff , and students ' actual experiences over definitions developed by outside parties . In this context the Harvard Task Force took cognisance of the three main definitions of antisemitism to which I have already referred but noted that ' when members of the Harvard community experience hateful conduct and exclusion , the problem is the conduct and the exclusion - not whether it maps onto a lengthy definition of antisemitism or any other form of bias ' . <p> 35.9 . Jewish students reported how October 7th profoundly affected their lives on campus . <p> 35.9.1 . A Jewish student reported : <p> ' Before October 7th , being Jewish was largely irrelevant . It was not a barrier . I was proud to be Jewish . When it came up , it was positive . After October 7th , I experienced the following in this order : first there was pressure , then there was chaos , then hostility , and in certain spaces , the normalization of subtle discrimination like , " We 'll welcome you in this space if you align in a certain way . If not , you ca n't come here . ' <p> 35.9.2 . Jewish students reported perceiving the word ' Zionist ' as being widely employed as a pejorative and a means of pillorying Jews : <p> ' There 's a tendency to replace the word Jewish with Zionist . " All Zionists should bum in hell " - it 's very frightening , there 's an incongruency between how different sides would define the term Zionist . People are proudly referring to themselves as anti-Zionist but there 's a strong overlap and it feels like this is an excuse to use a different term to make the point that they are anti-Jewish ' . <p> 35.9.3 . The Harvard Report notes that accounts of social shunning emerged as one of the most alarming indicators of the breakdown of Harvard 's on-campus community . <p> 35.9.3.1 . An undergraduate student reported : <p> ' The strategy used by BOS groups involves encouraging students and faculty to avoid normalizing relations with Jewish students . This involves social shaming , which is a particularly harmful tactic in academia and the impact can vary depending on your field . ' <p> 35.10 . The Harvard Report notes several concerning incidents of over antisemitism directed at students : <p> 35.10.1 . In October 2024 stickers bearing a swastika against the blue and white background of the Israeli flag and emblazoned with the words ' Stop Funding Israeli Terrorism ' , were found posted on the Harvard campus . <p> 35.10.2 . A Jewish student reported that being stalked across Harvard Yard by students whose faces were completely covered by keffiyehs , being screamed at while attending outdoor , on campus events held by Harvard Chabad , and having encountered ' more swastikas and classic antisemitic canards than I did growing up in the Bible Belt ' . <p> 35.10.3 . Another Jewish student reported being called a ' Palestinian-hating Zionist ' , despite never having publicly expressed any views on the conflict . The student stated ' maybe it was the fact that I wear a kippah that led them to believe that ' . <p> 36 . Despite the fact that more than 200 universities in the United Kingdom have adopted the IHRA definition of antisemitism , university campuses in the United Kingdom witnessed a 117% increase in antisemitic incidents across 30 campuses in the 2022-2024 academic years . <p> 37 . The United Kingdom based Community Safety Trust ( ' CST ' ) released its third biennial campus antisemitism report in December 2024 ( ' the CST Report ' ) . A copy of the report is annexed marked " WK7 " . <p> 37.1 . The CST Report notes that in the academic years covered by the report ( that is 2022- 2024 ) 325 university-related antisemitic incidents were recorded which is more than double the figure recorded in the previous report . <p> 37.2 . The CST Report notes that 53 incidents were recorded in 2022/2023 and 272 incidents were recorded in 2023/2024 - this is a record total for university-related antisemitic incidents and marks a 117% increase from the 150 university incidents recorded in 2020-2022 , the two academic years covered in CST 's previous report . <p> 37.3 . In 2023/2024 , the 272 university-related antisemitic incidents recorded by CST marked the highest total recorded for a single academic year . <p> 37.4. 81% of university-related incidents were categorised as Abusive Behaviour , totalling 264 incidents . This category includes all forms of verbal and written antisemitism both online and offline , other than those that include direct threats . 23 threats were made to Jewish students in the academic year 2023/2024 compared to 3 university ? related threats in 2022/2023 . <p> 37.5 . There were ten instances of assault , one occurring in the academic year 2022/2023 , and nine in 2023/2024 . One incident , involved eggs being thrown at a group of visibly Jewish students in St Andrews , while returning from an event with the Chief Rabbi . <p> 37.6 . There were 21 instances of damage and desecration of Jewish property , four occurring in the academic year 2022/2023 and 17 in 2023/2024 . <p> 37.7 . In one incident which took place during an event organised by the Medical Society and Law Society of their university , which included T ? shirt drawing , students wrote ' / heart Hitler ' , ' Jew ' and drew a caricatured Jewish face on the back of a known Jewish student 's T ? shirt . <p> 37.8 . In another incident , staff members in the sociology department at a university in the North of England had found copies of the antisemitic conspiracy newspaper in the foyer of the sociology department . This newspaper is notable for articles promoting white supremacists , Holocaust revisionism and defending explicit antisemitism . <p> 37.9 . In May 2024 , a Jewish university chaplain reported to CST that a visibly Jewish student had been told to leave an area due to their appearance . The student was wearing a kippah and walking on the other side of the road from the campus ' pro-Palestine student encampment when he bumped into friends and spoke for approximately a minute . At this point a private security guard told the student to move along because he was being ' provocative ' , despite no engagement with the encampment or its members . As a result , the student concluded that the only provocation the security guard could have been referring to was his wearing a kippah . <p> 38 . Similarly , campuses in Canada and the EU have all witnessed spikes in recorded incidents of antisemitism . Unfortunately , and as I will deal with in greater detail below . South African universities are not exempt from antisemitism disguised as anti-Zionism . I deal with the experiences of UCT students in the section below as well as the concerns regarding UCT 's perceived lack of response to complaints submitted with regard to these incidents . <p> ANTISEMITISM AT UCT <p> 39 . Both the respondents and the SAJFP seek to advance a narrative that UCT 's adoption of the IHRA definition of antisemitism would ' curtail protected speech and academic freedom , including legitimate criticism of the Israeli government and its military assault in Gaza ' . This narrative is , however , demonstrably false . Despite UCT having committed to the adoption of the IHRA definition when it signed the contract with the Donald Gordon Foundation ( ' DGF ' ) that is at issue in the main application , criticism of the Israeli government 's actions was prevalent on campus by both students and academics and remains so . <p> 40 . In the months immediately following the October 7th attacks , the SAJBD has witnessed a 631% increase in the number of recorded antisemitic incidents in South Africa . 2024 saw 140 recorded incidents making it the third highest year on record . In addition there has been a significant increase in the violence level involved in these incidents , which had been very rare in South Africa . Many of these incidents were reported on university campuses with UCT being especially prominent . The SAJBD 's antisemitism reports for 2023 and 2024 , show that out of all the universities which Jewish students attend across the <p> country , UCT has been reported as the university with the highest recorded number of antisemitic incidents at universities in the country . <p> 41 . Historically , Jewish students at UCT have been able to meaningfully engage with their counterparts regarding the Middle-East crisis . For years , members of SAUJS and the Palestinian Solidarity Forum ( ' PSF ' ) debated their respective positions in a healthy and constructive manner . However , in recent years , this has changed . <p> 42 . In March 2023 the PSF hosted an event at UCT where Hamas and Palestinian Islamic Jihad ( ' PIJ ' ) representatives addressed students via video call as part of that year 's Israel Apartheid Week ( ' IAW ) . It is a matter of public record that both Hamas and PIJ perpetrated the October 7th attacks . PIJ is considered to be one of the most extreme and uncompromising Palestinian armed factions . It rejects any political peace process and sees a military victory over Israel as the sole means of attaining its objective of establishing an Islamic state across Israel , the West Bank and Gaza . <p> 43 . One of the persons hosted by the PSF was Mr Nasser Abu Sharif ( ' Mr Sharif ) , the PIJ diplomatic representative in Iran . It bears emphasis that the PSF hosted the PIJ some 7 months before the October 7th attacks on Israel . <p> 44 . The SAJBD addressed correspondence to UCT 's Acting Vice Chancellor ( ' the Acting VC ' dated 20 March 2023 in which it drew the Acting VC 's attention to the fact that PIJ is widely considered to be a terrorist organisation and has been designated as such by countries throughout the world , including the United States , the European Union , the United Kingdom , Japan , Canada , Australia and New Zealand . Mr Sharif is on record as having not only incited violence against <p> Israel but has maligned Jews in the most egregious of terms . In the correspondence the SAJBD urged the Acting VC to consider the propriety and wisdom of allowing this talk to proceed and pointed out that SAUJS had been asked not to host a former IDF soldier on campus and that the same standard should apply to the PSF . A copy of the letter addressed to the Acting VC is annexed marked " WKS " . <p> 45 . On 21 March 2023 the SAJBD addressed a second letter to the Acting VC regarding the PSF event , a copy of which is annexed as " WK9 " . <p> 46 . In that letter the SAJBD outlined the sequence of events that took place as follows : <p> 46.1 . At 10h42 on 20 March 2023 , the then SAUJS UCT chairperson Ms Erin Dodo ( ' Ms Dodo ' ) called Ms Lindie Gayiza ( ' Ms Gayiza ' ) , who oversees IAW at UCT , to raise concerns about the proposed speaker as well as safety concerns for students on campus . Ms Gayiza requested that an email be sent to her manager , Ms Edwina Brooks , who would then investigate the matter . <p> 46.2 . An email was sent a short while later , in which the Acting VC was copied . <p> 46.3 . The only response received , however , was from the PSF , firstly apologizing for not providing the list of speakers timeously and then followed by an email stating PSF would not remove the speaker from the lineup . <p> 46.5 . Mr Bloch was told by Mr Tsotsobe that little could be done to stop the recorded message from airing due to time constraints . <p> 46.6 . The PSF event took place at 18h00 on 20 March 2023 . <p> 47 . The SAJBD drew the Acting VC 's attention to UCT 's rules in relation to invitations to speakers who are not staff members or students which state that : <p> ' Any person , department or other organisation wishing to arrange a meeting or other occasion at which a person who is not a member of staffor a student invited to speak , shall publish details of the meeting oroccasion and the identity of the speaker(s) in a prominent position onappropriate notice-boards and to which staff and students have access . <p> During the academic term , this publication shall be made available to therelevant staff and students as soon as the topic and , if possible . Thename ( s of the speaker(s) is ( are ) known . and as far as possible at leastseven clear working days before the scheduled time of the meeting oroccasion. notice of meeting time of notice : in academic term in the case of meetings scheduled during University vacations , such publication shall be made , whenever possible , seven clear working days during the term before the vacation in which the meeting is scheduled . Where this is impossible , the meeting should be publicized at least seven clear working days before it is scheduled to be held ' . emphasis added <p> 48 . The SAJBD pointed out that it was clear that UCT had more than enough time to prevent the recording of the PIJ as well as the presentation from Hamas from going ahead , but chose not to do so . The SAJBD also pointed out that UCT had denied SAUJS permission to host an event at which Mr Yosef Haddad ( ' Mr Haddad ' ) , an Arab Israeli Christian journalist , was to speak . <p> 49 . It is clear that UCT elected to permit the PSF event at which Hamas and PIJ were permitted to address students but it denied SAUJS permission to host Mr Haddad , presumably because he is supportive of Israel . The SAJBD pointed out to UCT that this amounts to an outright betrayal both of the right of Jewish students to fair and equal treatment and of the fundamental democratic , human rights values that UCT itself has traditionally adhered to . <p> 50 . To date there has been no response from UCT to the concerns and correspondence addressed by the SAJBD relating to this event in March 2023 . <p> 51 . On 20 June 2023 , the PSF issued a series of public statements posted on its social media sites celebrating the killing of four Israelis . One such post read - ' four settlers were horizontalized in a shooting ' . <p> 52 . The SAJBD sent a letter expressing its concerns to the Acting VC . A copy of the l.etter dated 28 June 2023 is annexed as " WK10 " . Once again these concerns were not meaningfully addressed by UCT . <p> 53 . In addition , the flags of Hamas , Hezbollah and PIJ which all have well documented history of antisemitic statements and ideologies quite apart from being recognised terrorist groups , have reportedly been seen on UCT campus , further intimidating Jewish students and further fostering an environment of hostility towards them . <p> 54 . The contract between UCT and the DGF , in which UCT expressly adopted the IHRA working definition of antisemitism , was concluded in September 2023 . <p> 55 . What followed the adoption of the IHRA working definition was not the curtailment of ' protected speech and academic freedom , including legitimate criticism of the Israeli government and its military assault in Gaza ' as alluded to by the respondents and the SAJFP . To the contrary , criticism of the Israeli government exponentially increased after September 2023 . <p> 56 . In September 2023 the PSF hosted a ' year-end event and evening of resistance ' in which it facilitated an online engagement between UCT students and a guest speaker Hezbollah spokesperson Ibrahim al-Mousawi . Hezbollah is designated as a terrorist group by Argentina , Australia , Austria , Bahrain , Canada , Colombia , Czech Republic , Estonia , Germany , the Gulf Cooperation Council , Guatemala , Honduras , Israel , Lithuania , Netherlands , New Zealand , Paraguay , Serbia , Slovenia , Switzerland , United Kingdom and USA . <p> 57 . On 7 October 2023 , Hamas invaded Israel , killing approximately 1 200 people , and kidnapping another approximately 250 . The vast majority of these people are Jewish . It is no coincidence that these terrorist attacks occurred on the Jewish Holy Day of Simchat Torah - a day where Jews celebrate the completion of the cycle of reading the Torah , and the beginning of a new cycle . <p> 58 . Following the October 7th attacks , and before any Israeli incursion into Gaza , the PSF posted on lnstagram that ' the time has come ' , clearly endorsing the <p> atrocities and encouraging others to engage in similar acts . The PSF further displayed Hamas and Hezbollah flags on campus and distributed sweets and cookies on campus to celebrate the deadliest attack against Jews since the Holocaust . <p> 59 . Rabbi Goldman in his affidavit which is filed with this affidavit describes attempting to engage with the PSF members , only to be verbally attacked and abused seemingly for his Jewishness . Rabbi Goldman describes seeing PSF members distributing cookies in the Palestinian flag colors with students waving the Hezbollah flag on campus 9 October 2023 . When he asked over a dozen of them if they condemn the murder , rapes and kidnapping by Hamas , not one of them did . He states that they all proudly parroted the same lines : ' we support the resistance ' and discouraged any members from talking with him . <p> 60 . Rabbi Goldman states further that there were also two other Jewish students with him , one of whom , upon hearing the lack of empathy of these students began crying . He describes how that student was made fun of by several students , with comments including ' go cry your white girl tears somewhere else ' . <p> 61 . UCT did not stop the PSF 's celebrations on 9 October 2023 , nor did it stop the PSF from calling for martyrdom and revolution in March 2024 during IAW . Although there had been verbal abuse , harassment and intimidation of Jewish students during IAW in previous years , the 2024 iteration of IAW marked a distinct escalation . <p> 62 . A Jewish student studying a Bachelor of Social Science majoring in Law and History has submitted an affidavit which is is annexed as " WK11 " . She has elected to submit her affidavit anonymously due to fear that she will face both <p> academic and social repercussions from her peers and members of the University itself , whether it be faculty or her classmates . <p> 63 . She describes her deep personal and profound connection to Israel as the grandchild of a Holocaust survivor which has shaped her understanding of the necessity for a Jewish homeland . <p> 64 . She describes being confronted during IAW 2024 by a female who held a ' F**ck Israel ' sign . She describes how the female continued to scream at her , to which in response she filmed her . This only agitated the person further , leading her to threatening to sue the student , calling her a ' dumb bitch ' and smacking her phone out of her hand . The person further threatened the student stating that she would hang photos of the student 's face around campus ' so , everyone could see who I was ' . <p> 65 . The student further describes how a fellow law student was standing next to her in a group and while laughing shouted ' F**k the Hostages ' . She describes how during IAW this year ( 2025 ) she was told by an Afrikaans male student that as a white South African she should be ' ashamed ' of her support for Israel , and that he further screamed at a group of Jewish students calling them ' pro apartheid ' , ' genocidal ' and ' Nazis ' . <p> 66 . Ms Rachel Segal ( ' Ms Segal ' ) the current Chairperson of SAUJS Western Cape , the representative student body for Jewish students in the region has deposed to an affidavit which is filed with this affidavit . Ms Segal states that for her the link between Judaism and Israel is inseparable in her lived experience . <p> 67 . She states that as a student leader , she has engaged with various levels of UCT administration and that she has observed a noticeable hesitancy from UCT to fully engage with or support Jewish student concerns . <p> 68 . Ms Segal relates that when she was engaging in debates during IAW 2024 , Hezbollah flags were raised , she and her fellow students were verbally assaulted by a massive crowd of students who hurled insults at them , insinuated that they would do to them what was done to Israelis on October 7th , threatened them and hurled antisemitic slurs at them . Shockingly she reports that there were UCT employees amongst the crowd of students . She reports that following this event , several peers avoided working with her on group work and engaging with her in tutorials . <p> 69 . She further states that since the impugned resolutions were adopted in 2024 , there has been an ' evident and measurable increase in antisemitic sentiment on campus ' . She reports having overheard students discussing how they would pull people 's kippas off their heads . She states that SAUJS has documented more than a dozen incidents in the past two years , including harassment , being spat on , doxxing , exclusion from events , vandalism of Jewish posters , and rhetoric conflating all Jews with Israeli government policies . <p> 70 . She is of the view that the resolutions have ' emboldened a climate where antisemitism is more easily excused under the banner of anti-Zionism . It has made Jewish students feel less safe and certainly less welcome at UCT ' . <p> 71 . Ms Dodo has also deposed to an affidavit which is filed with this affidavit . <p> 72 . In her affidavit Ms Dodo takes issue with Mr Sacks ' contention that SAUJS and the Kaplan Centre was hostile to SAJFP members or any other Jewish students and states that this simply does not reflect the lived experience of campus life . <p> 73 . In contrast Ms Dodo states that it was often SAUJS-affiliated students including her personally who faced open hostility , slurs , and marginalisation , particularly during politically charged periods like IAW . She states that ' the accusation that SAUJS created exclusionary environments is especially painful given that we were repeatedly placed in positions where our presence , identity , and political beliefs were not only challenged , but actively targeted ' . <p> 74 . Ms Dodo describes an incident that took place during IAW 2024 during which SAUJS hosted a peaceful , university-approved counter-event which was explicitly focused on fostering open dialogue and mutual understanding rather than confrontation . <p> 75 . Ms Dodo describes how in response to a verbal invitation from SAUJS members to SAJFP to join them in dialogue , SAJFP chose to proceed with their own unauthorised protest on campus , featuring speakers who made inflammatory and antisemitic remarks , including telling Jewish students to ' go back to Europe ' . <p> 76 . She describes how during that protest , she was personally harassed by Mr Jamie Rosengarten ( ' Mr Rosengarten ' ) , the then head of SAJFP . She states as follows : <p> ' At the time , I was standing calmly on Plaza speaking with members of the university administration , who had come to check in on the event . Without warning , Mr Rosengarten approached me <p> aggressively , shoved a camera in my face , and accused me of " crying crocodile tears . " The accusation was bizarre- I was not crying at a/l ? and clearly intended to provoke and discredit me . He then began shouting at me in front of others in what was clearly an attempt to intimidate and humiliate . I had to ask him repeatedly to calm down before he eventually stepped back . His conduct was threatening and inappropriate , and this was not an isolated incident ; he has a pattern of engaging in similarly confrontational behaviour . ' <p> 77 . Ms Dodo describes another disturbing incident which took place in November 2023 when she sent a neutral question in the course WhatsApp group asking whether there was any indication of when their essays would be returned . She states that in response , another student replied , ' When Palestineis free ' to which several classmates responded with agreement , emojis , <p> and affirmations . She describes how a few days later , following a vigil held on campus , the same group circulated a video of Professor Susan Levine speaking at the event . Immediately after , a classmate sent a message reading : <p> ' Anthropology is not a safe space for Zionists 9 . ' This message was ' liked ' by <p> other students . She states that the message was her directed at her , not only because of its timing , but because every student in that group knew she was the Chairperson of SAUJS and outspoken about her connection to Israel . <p> 78 . Ms Dodo states that the adoptions of the resolutions ' has exacerbated hostility towards Jewish students on campus . It emboldens those who use anti-Zionism as a smokescreen for antisemitism and reinforces the idea that Jewish students must disavow Israel to be accepted ' . She further states that resolutions ' may not have intended to legitimise such exclusion , but it has undeniably done so . It <p> has made the university environment more dangerous , not just politically divisive , but emotionally and psychologically unsafe for Jewish students ' . <p> 79 . Ms Talia Zalniker the Vice-Chairperson of SAUJS has also deposed to an affidavit which is filed together with this affidavit in which she states that her role as Vice-Chairperson of SAUJS makes her identity and views highly visible , and as a result , she has experienced judgmental looks and non-verbal hostility from fellow students . She describes a pervasive and rising climate of antisemitism on campus in her affidavit . <p> 80 . Further the SAJDB has received reports of Jewish students being physically assaulted whilst praying on campus ; Jewish students being spat at , and slapped ; anti-Israel protesters grabbing posters from Jewish students , ripping them up ; Jewish students displaying outward manifestations of their Judaism being verbally abused with taunts such as ' Child Killer ' , ' We are Hamas ' , ' October 7 will happen again ' . <p> 81 . In April 2024 , a fine arts student at UCT , Ylara Esau-Salie ( ' Ms Esau-Salie ' ) displayed an art work at UCT Plaza in which she had parodied a memorial plaque situated at the Auschwitz-Birkenau death camp in Poland . Nazi Germany operated five death camps , one of which was Auschwitz-Birkenau . <p> 82 . To provide context as to why Ms Esau-Salie 's artwork is antisemitic and deeply hurtful to Jewish people it is important to contextualise the Auschwitz concentration camp and the Auschwitz-Birkenau death camp . <p> 83 . The Auschwitz concentration camp was located on the outskirts of Oswiecim in German-occupied Poland . It was originally established in 1940 and later <p> referred to as ' Auschwitz I ' or ' Main Camp ' . Auschwitz main camp was a labour camp , where arriving Jewish prisoners faced a selection process . Those judged best able to work were selected for labour . Those who were not were sent immediately to the gas chambers at Auschwitz-Birkenau . <p> 84 . Auschwitz-Birkenau , also referred to as ' Auschwitz II ' was located near the Polish village Birkenau about 3 km from the Main Camp . Auschwitz-Birkenau played a central role in the German effort to kill the Jews of Europe . At the height of the deportations , an average of 6 000 Jews were gassed each day at the Auschwitz-Birkenau . By November 1944 , more than one million Jews and tens of thousands of Roma , Poles , and Soviet prisoners of war were killed there . <p> 85 . The text of the original memorial plaque at Auschwitz-Birkenau reads : <p> ' Forever let this place be a cry of despair and a warning to Humanity , where the Nazis murdered about one and a half million men , women , and children , mainly Jews from various countries of Europe . Auschwitz ? Birkenau 1940-1945 ' <p> 86 . Ms Esau-Salie created a replica of the memorial plaque , but altered the text to read : <p> ' Forever let this place be a cry of despair and a warning to Humanity , where ISRAEL murdered about 28000 men , women , and children , mainly MUSLIM from GAZA . PALESTINE 1948- ' <p> 87 . She then called on students to adopt the Jewish memorial custom of placing pebbles on the plaque , which she titled , ' Never Again ' . A copy of Ms Esau ? Salie 's work is annexed hereto and marked " WK12 " . <p> 88 . It is apparent from the image annexed above that Ms Esau-Salie placed : <p> 88.1. the word ' Israel ' over the words ' the Nazis ' ; <p> 88.2. the number ' 28 000 ' over the words ' one and a half million ' ; <p> 88.3. the word ' Muslim ' over the word ' Jews ' ; <p> 88.4. the word ' Gaza ' over ' various countries of Europe ' , <p> 88.5. the word ' Palestine ' over the words ' Auschwitz-Birkenau ' ; and <p> 88.6. the date ' 1948- ' over the date ' 1940-1945 ' , <p> 89 . The clear and disturbing implications of Ms Esau-Salie 's work which she displayed on the UCT Plaza as well as publishing online , is to equate Israel with the Nazis . <p> 90 . The PSF praised Ms Esau-Salie 's work , posting that the artwork was not ' meant to offend anyone besides the genocide supporters and Zionists in disguise . If the shoe fits , wear it ' , and ' Reject normalisation , support the resistance : we do n't want peace , we want freedom . We do n't want to live side-by-side , we want the settlers to leave our land . There 's no conversation to be had . ' <p> 91 . Copies of these posts are annexed marked " WK13 " . <p> 92 . The Cape SAJDB addressed correspondence to UCT a copy of which is annexed as " WK14 " in which it states the following : <p> ' The Cape SAJBD believes in freedom of expression and the freedom of the creative arts . However , this student has crossed the lines between innovative artwork and that which is offensive and hurtful . The use of the Holocaust plaque is highly insensitive and is disrespectful to the memory of the six million Jews who were systematically executed by the Nazis . Whilst we mourn all loss of life and believe the deaths of Palestinian civilians is tragic , it does not give anyone the right to diminish the atrocities of the Holocaust . It would be more uplifting if provocative art was designed to promote peace and healing , instead of antagonizing and causing hurt ' . <p> 93 . Ms Esau-Salie has to the best of my knowledge not been disciplined or even subjected to any disciplinary process by UCT . <p> 94 . Professor Mendelsohn speaking in his capacity as the Director of the Kaplan Centre aptly described the overt antisemitism of Ms Esau-Salie 's work stating : <p> ' The ' artwork 's ' message is doubly offensive . It invokes and literally overwrites the sacred memory of those who died at Auschwitz-Birkenau . And it lays the terrible price of the war in Gaza at the feet of the Jewish people - not Hamas , not Israel , but Jews . Given that UCT commits to principles and policies designed to advance inclusivity and respect and is otherwise intolerant of prejudice , I trust that the university will take appropriate action . ' <p> 95 . The aforementioned attacks on Jewish students , Ms Esau-Salie 's work , and the actions of PSF are clear examples of antisemitism , and on all-fours of the approach adopted by the Constitutional Court in Masuku . The Jewish students were not targeted simply because they are pro-Zionist . They were targeted because they are both pro-Zionist and Jewish . UCT did not prevent these actions from taking place , nor did it ' curtail protected speech and academic freedom , including legitimate criticism of the Israeli government and its military assault in Gaza ' . To the contrary , it endorsed and protected the behaviour . <p> 96 . In this regard , the SAJBD sent repeated letters of complaint to UCT via its Vice Chancellor regarding the increasing incidents targeting Jewish students . I annex copies of these letters hereto as " WK15 " . <p> 97 . However , despite UCT 's assurances in a letter dated 13 March 2024 that such conduct was to be ' condemned in the strongest terms and action will be taken against individuals who perpetrate such actions ' , no such actions were taken and the targeting of Jewish students has continued unabated . A copy of UCT 's letter dated 13 March 2024 is annexed hereto and marked " WK16 " . <p> 98 . Jewish academics have also faced attack and unwarranted scrutiny from their colleagues at UCT . Apart from Professor Mendelsohn , the SAJBD has been approached by a member of the humanities faculty at UCT , Professor Elisa Galgut ( ' Professor Galgut ' ) , who has provided an affidavit detailing her experience at UCT during the time the two resolutions were adopted . Her affidavit is filed with this affidavit . <p> 99 . Professor Galgut in her affidavit describes how she was effectively silenced by fellow staff members when trying to engage in a debate regarding whether Israel is committing genocide in Gaza . <p> 100 . What is abundantly clear from both Professor Mendelsohn and Professor Galgut 's experiences is that UCT did not meaningfully conduct any kind of investigation , debate or discourse prior to adopting the resolutions . Instead , those who wished to engage in a debate were silenced , ridiculed and ostracised . <p> 101 . A similar experience was faced by Dr Sheri Hanson ( " DR Hanson ) , albeit in 2011 and when she was a resident professor at the University of Johannesburg ( ' UJ ' ) . I annex hereto as " WK17 " an affidavit deposed to by Professor Hanson in this regard . <p> 102 . Dr Hanson similarly explains that , prior to UJ implementing its academic boycott of Ben Gurion University , ' no opportunity was provided for alternative viewpoints to be heard or for a balanced discussion of the complex issues involved ' . Professor Hanson explains that UJ 's boycott ' created an atmosphere where I felt my identity as a Jewish person was being implicitly targeted and marginalized within the academic community ' . Her experiences resulted in her leaving academia . <p> 103 . The targeting of Jews on the UCT campus is so prevalent that Jewish students , staff and faculty have become less willing to openly identify as Jewish . This may be in terms of their physical identity , or through their work and choice to participate in lectures or campus . In 2025 there has been a major shift in the choices of study of Jewish students in the Western Cape , with many choosing Stellenbosch University over UCT . Reports indicate that the number of Jewish <p> students at Stellenbosch tripled in 2024 . Accounts from the student body indicates that hostility against Jewish students at UCT and a perceived insecurity that this causes is a major reason for this shift . In this regard I annex copies of two recent South African Jewish Report articles : <p> 104 . Zionism is defined as the movement advocating for the return of the Jewish people to their ancestral homeland , and the continued support of a sovereign Jewish state in Israel . Accordingly Zionism is , by its very definition , intertwined and enmeshed with Judaism . <p> 106 . The survey , the most comprehensive of its kind , found that 69% of South African Jews self-identify as Zionists . <p> 107 . Additionally , 92% affirm Israel as the ancestral homeland of the Jewish people , 89% have visited Israel at least once ( with nearly one in five having visited over <p> ten times ) , and 66% report a strong attachment to Israel , with an additional 24% moderately attached , so that together 90% of South African Jewry feel an attachment to the State if Israel . <p> 108 . By contrast , the same survey found only 4% of respondents having a ' weak attachment ' to Israel while a mere 1% described themselves as having ' no attachment ' at all . <p> 109 . These findings demonstrate an enduring connection to Israel within the South African Jewish community , underscoring the relevance of Zionist identity in understanding the community 's self-image . <p> 110 . A copy of the survey is annexed as " WK20 " <p> 111 . This is acutely evident from the affidavits delivered by the UCT students who express a deep and personal connection between their identities as Jewish and as Zionist . Historically , too , the world has seen Zionism and Judaism as inextricably intertwined . <p> 112 . In order to understand and contextualise the connection between the creation of a Jewish state and Jewish identity post World War II it is necessary to understand the historical context of the establishment of the State of Israel . <p> 113 . The Jewish presence in the land historically known as Eretz Yisrael ( the Land of Israel ) has been continuous for millennia . Despite periods of exile , foreign rule , and dispersion , Jewish communities remained in cities such as Jerusalem , Safed , Tiberias , and Hebron , known as the Four Holy Cities , throughout antiquity and the medieval period . Jewish religious texts and liturgy throughout <p> the diaspora consistently expressed longing for a return to the Land of Israel and the rebuilding of Jerusalem . This spiritual and historical connection laid the foundation for Jewish nativism - the belief in the indigenous and ancestral ties of the Jewish people to the land of Israel . <p> 114 . The emergence of political Zionism in the late 19th century responded to growing antisemitism in Europe but was also fuelled by a deeper historical drive to return to the ancestral homeland . Early waves of Jewish immigrants , or olim , arrived not only from Eastern Europe but also from Yemen , Iraq , Persia ( Iran ) , and North Africa . By the early 20th century , Jews from both Ashkenazi and Sephardi/Mizrahi backgrounds were actively participating in rebuilding a national home . <p> 115 . In the aftermath of World War II , the Jewish people were left with immense suffering , trauma , and an acute understanding that they needed to find a way to secure their future . The Holocaust it made clear that Jews , wherever they lived , were vulnerable to antisemitism and that antisemitism led to persecution and ultimately the almost near annihilation of the Jewish population in Europe . The Holocaust highlighted the vulnerability of Jews living in Europe without a sovereign state to protect them . The horrors of the Holocaust demonstrated the perilous position of Jews in a world where , despite centuries of contributions to various societies , they were still scapegoated , persecuted , and ultimately annihilated by a brutal ideology . <p> 116 . In 1933 , approximately 9.5 million Jews lived in Europe , comprising more than 60% of the world 's Jewish population at that time . It is estimated that in 1939 worldwide who face discrimination or violence , providing a sanctuary for those in need . <p> 120 . An equally significant and often under-acknowledged movement of Jews took place from regions other than Europe . Between the late 1940s and the 1970s , between 850 000 and 1 million Jews were displaced or fled from across the Arab region and Africa , including Iraq , Yemen , Egypt , Libya , Syria , Lebanon , Iran , Morocco , Tunisia , and Algeria . These communities were often centuries old , with deep ties to local cultures . Despite this , growing antisemitism , discriminatory laws , state-sponsored persecution , and popular violence intensified following the establishment of the State of Israel in 1948 . In many instances , Jews were stripped of their citizenship , had property confiscated , faced riots and violence , and were subjected to discriminatory laws . <p> 120.1 . In Iraq following the 1941 Farhud pogrom in Baghdad and worsening persecution as a result of later government decrees , nearly the entire Iraqi Jewish population numbering over 130 000 was left Iraq between 1948 and 1951 . Most were airlifted to Israel during Operation Ezra and Nehemiah . <p> 120.2 . Between 1948 to September 1950 , close to 450 flights were chartered , in what became known as ' Operation Magic Carpet ' airlifting some 48 000 Yemeni Jews , almost the entire Jewish community , as well as 500 Jews from Djibouti and Eritrea and some 2 000 Jews from Saudi Arabia , to Israel via Aden . <p> 120.3 . In Egypt , after 1948 and particularly following the 1956 Suez Crisis , Jews were arrested , expelled , and stripped of citizenship and property . Over 50 000 Jews subsequently fled Egypt to Israel during this period . <p> 120.4 . In Libya , violent pogroms in 1945 and 1948 led to mass emigration . <p> By 1967 , nearly all of Libya 's 38 000 Jews had left , mostly for Israel . <p> 120.5 . Between the 1950s and 1960s , as Morocco , Tunisia , and Algeria gained independence from colonial rule , nationalist movements in these countries often targeted Jews . Over 300 000 Jews from the Maghreb region relocated to Israel during this period . <p> 120.6 . Before the 1979 Islamic Revolution , Iran had a large Jewish population . After the revolution , rising persecution and anti-Zionist sentiment forced tens of thousands of Iranian Jews to emigrate , many to Israel . <p> 120.7 . In India the Bene Israel , Cochin , and Baghdadi Jewish communities began immigrating to Israel in the 1950s . Though not persecuted , many were drawn by the opportunity to be part of the Jewish state . <p> 120.8 . Ethiopian Jews , known as Beta Israel , were brought to Israel through Operation Moses ( 1984 ) and Operation Solomon ( 1991 ) , rescuing tens of thousands from famine and war . <p> 121 . These immigrants from known as Mizrahi and Sephardi Jews were instrumental in shaping Israel 's demographic and cultural identity . Despite early socioeconomic and other challenges they have contributed immensely to <p> Israel 's economy , political landscape , social , and spiritual life . Their integration remains a central chapter in the ongoing process of nation-building in Israel . These mass movements reaffirm the deeply rooted and diverse character of Jewish civilization and the centrality of Israel as a place of refuge , renewal , and return for Jews from across the globe . <p> 122 . Criticism of the existence of Israel , especially when it questions the fundamental right of the Jewish people to have a state of their own often veers into the realm of antisemitism . The criticism of Israel 's existence is frequently rooted in the denial of the Jewish people 's historical connection to their homeland and their right to self-determination . This is not merely a political critique but an existential denial of Jews ' fundamental right to exist as a people with a sovereign state . When such criticism crosses this line , it becomes a form of antisemitism because it challenges the very identity and survival of the Jewish people . Anti ? Zionism is a term carrying manifold and blurred dimensions ; yet to advocate for the active dissolution of the world 's only Jewish state is quite different from even the bitterest critique of its policies . <p> 123 . It is thus no coincidence that many of the historic attacks on the State of Israel have occurred on or during Jewish holidays . These include the Yorn Kippur war of 1973 , and the October 7th attacks . <p> 124 . Hamas ' original 1988 Charter speaks of Hamas ' struggle against ' the Jews ' and is a deeply antisemitic document . <p> 124.1 . By way of example in Article 7 of the original Hamas Charter it is states : <p> ' Moreover , if the links have been distant from each other and if obstacles , placed by those who are the lackeys of Zionism in the way of the fighters obstructed the continuation of the struggle , the Islamic Resistance Movement aspires to the realisation of Allah 's promise , no matter how Jong that should take . The Prophet , Allah bless him and grant him salvation , has said : <p> " The Day of Judgement will not come about until Mos/ems fight the Jews ( killing the Jews ) , when the Jew will hide behind stones and trees . The stones and trees will say O Mos/ems , 0 Abdulla , there is a Jew behind me , come and kill him . Only the Gharkad tree , ( evidently a certain kind of tree ) would not do that because it is one of the trees of the Jews . " ( related by a/ ? Bukhari and Moslem ) . ' <p> 124.2 . Article 20 of the original Hamas Charter states : <p> ' In their Nazi treatment , the Jews made no exception for women or children . Their policy of striking fear in the heart is meant for all . They attack people where their breadwinning is concerned , extorting their money and threatening their honour . They deal with people as if they were the worst war criminals . Deportation from the homeland is a kind of murder . ' <p> 125 . Although Hamas issued a later Charter in 2017 it has never expressly or formally repudiating the original 1988 Charter <p> 126 . That said , the SAJBD recognises that the support of the Jewish People 's right to self-determination in their ancestral homeland , is not necessarily synonymous with blind support of the policies and decisions of the current Israeli government . A simple conspectus of the Israeli Knesset reveals this fallacy of conflating Zionism with a defence of the Israeli government 's policies and decision . The current Knesset is made up of 120 members , of which no single party won more than 32 seats . The ruling government is made up of a coalition of six separate parties . <p> 127 . The oversimplification of Zionism as a blind loyalty to the current Israeli government is reductive and insulting to the vast majority of South African Jews who legitimately hold Zionist ( but not necessary pro-Israeli government ) views . <p> 128 . The South African Jewish community is a heterogenous one , with its members having a wide array of beliefs and opinions , including regarding Israel . It is all but inevitable that within its ranks there will be some who are anti-Zionist . As the evidence and statistics cited above clearly shows , however , they constitute a very small minority of Jewish opinion . <p> 129 . One can ( and many do ) legitimately criticise Israel 's actions and policies without necessarily being antisemitic . It is , however , where criticism of Israel 's actions and policies is combined with a targeted attack on a person simply for being Jewish , that this strays from legitimate criticism to blatant antisemitism . <p> 130 . This has been clearly explained by the Constitutional Court in Masuku where the Constitutional Court held : - <p> ' In respect of the first statement , the Equality Court concluded that the words were based on membership of the Jewish religion or ethnicity . The Supreme Court of Appeal made its finding in terms of a separate area of the law , so it can be put to one side . The Equality Court concluded as it did , because the majority of Jewish people are Zionists in South Africa and globally . Although this may not be ordinarily sufficient to find that the remarks were based on the Jewish identity - as that would mean every mention of Zionism may potentially open itself to censure - the Equality Court went on to conclude that the further reference to " Hitler ' ' made it clear that the statement was based on Jewish ethnicity . The Equality Court held that a reasonable reader would have noted that a reference to Hitler to a group that was predominately Jewish was used because of their " Jewishness " - namely , their Jewish ethnicity and identity . As the Equality Court noted , Hitler 's anti-Semitic extermination campaign was not limited to people of the Jewish faith or ethnicity who identified as Zionists . Moreover , any mention of " Hitler ' ' undeniably evokes semantic associations with the entire global Jewish community , and not a specific faction thereof . ' <p> 131 . One only need scroll through X ( formerly Twitter ) see how the criticism of Israel is , more often than not , combined with clear and rampant antisemitism . I attach hereto as " WK21 " examples of such Twitter posts : - <p> 131.1 . ' If we all die , I just want you to now that it was because of the jews ' ; <p> 131.2 . ' Next to the devil , a Chirstian has no more bitter enemy that the Jew . <p> Nobody else causes us as much suffering as these base children of the devil , this race of reptiles . These poisonous , envenomed worms should be drafted into forced labor , or they should simply be kicked out for all time ' . <p> 131.3 . ' F**king Jews ... the Jews are responsible for all the wars in the world ' . <p> 131.4 . ' Isolate the fifty richest Jews , and all wars will cease ' . <p> 131.5 . ' NEVER FORGET THE JEWS STARTED THIS ' . <p> 131.6 . ' Virtually no actual Americans are for the war with Iran ... only Jews are . Ergo . Jews are not Americans , they are just Jews ' . <p> 131.7 . ' F**k Israel . F**k the jews ' . <p> 131.8 . ' These jews just cant help themselves , they attack Iran unprovoked and they 'll be crying about how they do n't feel safe by morning ' . <p> 132 . This is precisely the hate speech that is fostered when institutions like UCT create a climate where anti-Zionist and anti-Israel sentiment can be used with impunity as an excuse to perpetrate antisemitism . <p> 133 . The respondents and SAJFP contend that the IHRA definition seeks to prevent ' legitimate criticism of the Israeli government and its military assault in Gaza ' . Yet , as has been articulated above , criticism of Israel and the conflict in Gaza was prevalent at UCT both before and after it had adopted the IHRA definition , and remains prevalent at various universities which retain the IHRA definition . <p> 134 . While the SAJFP positions itself as an alternative voice to the SAJBD , in reality it is a small representation of the South African Jewish Community , that the SAJBD estimates to be no more 150 members . <p> CONCLUSION AND REMEDY <p> 135 . The SAJBD intends to make submissions demonstrating the impact of the impugned resolutions on the lived reality of Jewish students and faculty members at UCT . <p> 136 . The impugned resolutions were adopted without considering their impact on Jewish students and faculty members . There was no consultation with Jewish students , faculty or staff . When Jewish faculty ( such as Professor Galgut and Professor Mendelsohn ) attempted to engage on the issue , they were ridiculed and ostracised . <p> 137 . The result is that UCT has become an unsafe place for Jewish students , staff and faculty , who face constant psychological , emotional and sometimes physical attacks . The adoption of the resolutions has only legitimized and sanitized these attacks creating an atmosphere that has a clear harmful and discriminatory impact on Jewish students <p> 138 . This was the situation before the adoption of the impugned resolutions , and remains the situation to this day . The impugned resolutions have had a detrimental impact on the academic choices of students at UCT , and driven many students and staff to seek safety elsewhere . <p> 139 . The SAJBD contends that its submissions and the evidence that it intends to place before the Court will assist the Court in reaching its judgment and creating appropriate rights based jurisprudence which gives rise to substantive equality by acknowledging the discriminatory impact of the impugned resolutions on Jewish members of the UCT community and placing* their lived reality at the forefront of implementing laws in a manner which is in line with our Constitutional obligations and obligations in terms of international laws . <p> 140 . The SAJBD therefore seeks an order in terms of the Notice of Motion to which this affidavit is attached .

Image : THILO SCHMUELGEN/Reuters/ File photo <p> Brazil is finalising its submission to join SA 's genocide case against Israel 's actions in Gaza at the International Court of Justice , the foreign ministry said in a statement on Wednesday . <p> SA filed a case in 2023 asking the ICJ to declare that Israel was in breach of its obligations under the 1948 Genocide Convention . The case argues that in its war against Hamas militants , Israel 's military actions go beyond targeting Hamas alone by attacking civilians , with strikes on schools , hospitals , camps and shelters . <p> Other countries -- including Spain , Turkey and Colombia -- have also sought to join the case against Israel . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ of violations of international law , " such as the annexation of territories by force " , and it expressed " deep indignation " at violence suffered by the civilian population . <p> Israel denies deliberately targeting Palestinian civilians , saying its sole interest is to annihilate Hamas . Lawyers for Israel have dismissed SA 's case as an abuse of the genocide convention . <p> The Israeli embassy in Brasilia said the Brazilian statement used " harsh words that do not fully portray the reality of what is happening in Gaza , " while Brazil also " completely ignored " the role of Hamas within Gaza 's reality . <p> Brazil 's National Israeli association CONIB said in a statement in response to Wednesday 's decision : " The breaking of Brazil 's long-standing friendship and partnership with Israel is a misguided move that proves the extremism of our foreign policy " . <p> Brazilian President Luiz Inacio Lula da Silva has long been an outspoken critic of Israel 's actions in Gaza , but Wednesday 's decision carries added significance amid heightened tensions between Brazil and Israel ally @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ all Brazilian goods this month . <p> A diplomat familiar with the thinking of the Lula administration told Reuters that Brazil does not believe its decision to join SA 's case will affect its relationship with Washington . <p> The US has opposed SA 's genocide case under both Democratic former President Joe Biden and Trump , a Republican . In February , Trump signed an executive order to cut US financial assistance to SA , citing in part its ICJ case . 
@@127354240 <h> Key topics : <p> Alleged ties between Gift of the Givers and Islamist-linked organisations <p> Concerns over Sooliman 's political influence and foreign affiliations <p> Sign up for your early morning brew of the BizNews Insider to keep you up to speed with the content that matters . Register here . <p> If you prefer WhatsApp for updates , sign up to the BizNews channel here . <p> The auditorium doors will open for BNIC#2 on 10 September 2025 in Hermanus . For more information and tickets , click here . <p> By Ilan Preskovsky* <p> In his last State of the Union , President Ramaphosa announced the formation of a " National Dialogue " . A strange name for an initiative , as the phrase " national dialogue " usually evokes images of South Africans gathering together to complain about the ANC , but in this @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ thirty-or-so South African thought leaders from all walks of life that will meet mid-August to brainstorm ideas on how to " fix " the many issues facing South Africa today . Cynically , and not incorrectly , this could easily be written off as the ANC , once again , outsourcing its responsibilities -- and by extension , it 's blame -- to someone else , but honestly , considering how it has been private individuals and companies that have been carrying the slack for our increasingly hopeless leaders for years , it 's probably not a terrible idea . <p> What may be a terrible idea , though , is who the president decided to appoint as one of the " eminent persons " of the conference , which is due to be held on 15 August : Dr. Imtiaz Sooliman , the founder and head of Gift of the Givers . <p> I know . But before getting into why Dr Sooliman may be a worrying choice to play a large part of this initiative ( there seems to be conflicting reports about whether he is co-chairing it @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ it ) , I 'm fully aware that I am jumping head-first into shark infested waters with bits of bloody steak sticking out of my pockets . Sooliman is an extremely well respected individual in this country and Gift of the Givers is about as beloved as institutions get , and not without good reason . Criticising either Sooliman or his organisation that has done so much to provide disaster relief worldwide is tricky , but all the more so when it is all but guaranteed to be written off as a " Zionist smear campaign " to discredit someone who has never been shy about his hatred towards Israel and Zionism . <p> But to reiterate what I said when I last broached the subject , pointing out concerns with Sooliman and/or Gift of the Givers no more discredits all the good that has been done in their name than acknowledging that good erases any wrongs . And though it would be a stretch to say there is any one smoking gun that would prove beyond a doubt that Sooliman and his NPO have been pursuing nefarious ends , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ on a clear-eyed view of Sooliman and his associations . <h> A List of Wrongs <p> In a previous article , based on the findings presented in an open letter on the Times of Israel by attorney and former chairman of the Jewish Board of Deputies , Lawrence Nowosenetz , I outlined several very specific concerns with how Gift of the Givers operates , how it is associated with various Islamic charities that have been used as fronts for funding terror , and how its founder and head continuously spouts views that are the antithesis of what a liberal democracy like South Africa is supposed to stand for . <p> I broke down the close association between Gift of the Givers and the umbrella charity Union of Good , which has been sanctioned by the US for funnelling funds to radical Islamist groups like Hamas , and a new version of which launched in South Africa under the direction of one Imtiaz Sooliman . I spoke of how Gift of the Givers created hospitals in Gaza that have been used as military bases by Hamas , while also handing out @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ took a deep dive into Sooliman himself ; how he was greatly influenced by Jihadi thinkers like Sheik Yusuf al Qaradawi , how he has publicly stated that he cares only about Sharia law and boasted about breaking secular laws on numerous occasions . That he has openly stood with Hamas against Israel and has been instrumental in pushing the ANC in its court case against the Jewish state at the International Court of Justice , almost goes without saying . <p> These allegations against Sooliman and Gift of the Givers have appeared on several different platforms , and his response to all of them has been very telling . He denied any malfeasance , of course , on the part of Gift of the Givers , but crucially , he failed to distance himself from either his inflammatory rhetoric or his Islamist associations , and doubled down on his hatred of Israel and mistrust of Zionists . He then released the following statement : <p> " Gift of the Givers would like to thank lawyer Lawrence , Times of Israel , Feinberg and the SA Jewish Report , Alec @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ incredible escalation in our profile both locally and internationally on platforms we did n't even know existed . This has resulted in a flood of current and new donors wanting to support our various causes . You have benefited the Palestinian cause immensely . " <p> Cute , but as near as I can tell , Gift of the Givers has not undergone an external forensic audit since these allegations first came out and Sooliman has not been held to account for , well , anything . Indeed , according to a recent report , over the past twelve months , Sooliman has gone to a lot of trouble to ensure that he is more untouchable than ever by increasingly entrenching himself in South Africa 's media and political institutions . <p> As the report puts it , " Over the past twelve months , Sooliman has significantly accelerated his effort to personally embed himself within the top echelons of South African media . These engagements are notably not incidental ; they are targeted acts of soft influence , timed with geopolitical developments , and executed with a precision more @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ With Political Clout <p> This is something that has been conspicuous about Sooliman from the start : he has frequently blurred the lines between political actor and philanthropist , and rather than allowing the often incredible work done by his organisation to speak for itself , his influence on setting the , well , national discourse , has been pretty unrelenting . <p> Sooliman first arrived on the scene not as a philanthropist , but as a politician - in particular , as one of the founders of the African Muslim Party . The AMP , with its platform drawn from Sharia law , was never more than a fringe political party that won zero seats in the 1994 election and since then had only fractionally better success in the Western Cape . But Sooliman has been playing politics ever since , albeit with an almost singular obsession with demonizing Israel . <p> Sooliman has also seldom had a larger profile than the past year -- and especially since the allegations against him were first raised . In particular , he has publicly met with the publishers , editors , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ media houses ; has become increasingly involved with the South African Police Service ; and has been in ever closer contact with political actors from Turkey and Qatar . These are n't just strange activities for the head of a disaster relief organisation to engage in , especially so publicly , but point towards something genuinely worrying . <p> His rapid succession of direct and ( after the public photo ops ) closed-door meetings with South Africa 's mainstream media points less towards the usual sort of media relations employed by an NPO -- public press releases and advertising , usually handled by the NPO 's marketing manager rather than its CEO -- than towards a concerted effort to mould the narrative around him . <p> His ingratiation with SAPS , though , is even more bizarre as it has less than nothing to do with the work in which he or his organisation are involved . And again , why so publicly ? It 's hard to see this as anything but a display of his influence ; perhaps even of his impunity and power . Presenting himself as @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ both the media and the police at the very least demolishes the idea of his being an apolitical philanthropist , but may point towards someone who desperately needs to control the entire narrative around him . <p> Which , inevitably , brings us to his engagement and close relationships with political representatives from Qatar and Turkey . Obviously , some engagement with Middle Eastern countries is entirely appropriate for the head of a humanitarian organisation that works extensively in these areas , but that it 's these two countries , in particular , is significant . As is the fact that his engagement with them rivals and surpasses that of South Africa 's official diplomatic representatives . <p> Turkey , under the dictatorial leadership of President Recep Tayyip Erdogan , has been moving steadily away from its Western affiliations in past years and aligning itself more firmly with the region 's more hard-line , anti-Western Islamist movements ; chief among them the Muslim Brotherhood . <p> Qatar , meanwhile , has become one of the most important and most dangerous players on the world stage . The country itself is @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ a large underclass of actual , bonafide slaves -- and its own close association with the Muslim Brotherhood and its most notorious offshoot , Hamas . But because it is extremely wealthy , it has been exerting an ever increasing influence on the West , pouring billions of dollars into Western governments , institutions and education establishments . Just recently , and most visibly , President Donald Trump was rightly criticised for accepting a 400 million dollar aeroplane from the Qataris . <p> If this anti-Western , anti-democratic , totalitarian Islamist dictatorship that employs actual slaves can exerts this much power on the United States , just imagine what it could do to a vulnerable liberal democracy like South Africa . And then imagine just why someone who has openly shown disdain for secular law and has a seething , seemingly bottomless hatred for the State of Israel , might find himself as South Africa 's unofficial point man for Qatar and Turke <h> Impunity , Immunity and Doubt <p> No doubt , Sooliman and his supporters will brush all of this off as mere co-incidence , at best , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ also once again insist that these claims be taken to the appropriate authorities . Whatever else you might say about him , Imtiaz Sooliman is no one 's fool . He has always been very careful to play things just on the right side of a reasonable doubt ; leaving plenty of smoke but precious little fire . And in case anyone does happen to notices just how much smoke there is around him , and starts to put 2 and 2 together , he has been very sure to make himself virtually untouchable . <p> But just because Sooliman has assured that these allegations against him are never quite solid enough to stand up in court and has cultivated relationships with just the right people to ensure that they do n't stand up in the court of public opinion either , does n't mean that he should be representing a forum dedicated to mapping the future of South Africa .

@@127357482 <h> Silent call to conscience on Mandela Day <h> For the members of the public and organisations who held a silent protest outside the Durban Holocaust and Genocide Centre on Nelson Mandela Day , their silence definitely spoke volumes with many taking note . The protesters let their placards with strong messages such as " Open aid routes into Gaza " and " Food is not a weapon " speak for them , in their call for peace . <p> ON Friday , a silent picket took place outside the Durban Holocaust and Genocide Centre . For 67 minutes , people stood in silence . <p> There were no chants , no speeches , no shouting . Just the presence of human beings standing still in protest , each one holding a message for the world to see . The message was clear , " Open aid routes into Gaza " . <p> There were no chants , no speeches , and no shouting , just the presence of human beings standing still in protest . Photo : Wendy Sithole <p> " This Mandela Day , a coalition of South @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ act . We chose silence as our form of protest , because the world has been too quiet about Gaza for too long . As people are starved , bombed , and erased , global leaders offer words , but little else . Silence became our tool to reflect the failure of those in power . It also became our expression of deep grief , rage , and moral duty , " shared Nasiha Soomar , spokesperson of #FridaysForPalestine . <p> " We stood in solidarity with Gaza because Mandela once reminded us that our freedom as South Africans is incomplete without the freedom of the Palestinian people . That truth remains unchanged . " The picket was supported by The South Africa Palestine Movement , The National Association of Democratic Lawyers , People Against Oppression , Active Citizens Movement , R.A.U.F. , Al Sirat and several others who believe that justice must be seen and felt . <p> The placards were loud and clear in their call to allow aid into Gaza . Photo : Wendy Sithole <p> " These are not political groups alone . These are people @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ profit , or alliances . The location of the picket carried its own weight . The Holocaust and Genocide Centre is a place of memory and warning . It teaches us what happens when humanity looks away . To gather outside that building and demand action for Gaza is to say that we have not forgotten what silence can cost . <p> " This Mandela Day , we did not light candles or hand out soup . We stood , silently , for the people of Gaza , because justice delayed is justice denied . Because humanity must come first , " concluded Soomar . <p> It 's the little paper with a big heart that visits your home every week delivering local news that affects you and connects you with local advertisers to help you make the best possible , pocket-friendly shopping choices . @@127468965 <h> DOCUMENTS <h> This is a new scramble for Africa - Cyril Ramphosa <p> ANC President says that for centuries , continent has paid the price for rapid industrial development of global North <p> Unrevised transcript of ANC President Cyril Ramaphosa address to the Summit of Liberation Movements , Kempton Park , 27 July 2025 <p> Leaders and representatives of our liberation movements , youth and women leagues of our liberation movements , elders and veterans invited , leaders and representatives from fraternal parties , members of the diplomatic corps , delegates and of course members of the media . It is with a great deal of honour , revolutionary pride and heartfelt gratitude that I greet you all on behalf of the African National Congress , the hosts of this summit and the people of South Africa . The Liberation Movement Summit is more than a conference . <p> It is what one would call a homecoming . It is a convocation of liberation movements rooted in shared histories of struggle bound by a tradition of resistance and united in a future that demands memory , imagination , boldness @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ by fraternal organisations and parties from across the world who share the goals of liberation and freedom from oppression . <p> Together our movements were forged in the cauldrons of anti-apartheid and anti-colonial struggle . Today , together , we must be forged anew in the fire of a new struggle . The struggle for social and economic justice for our people , regional unity , integration and sovereignty in an increasingly hostile global order . <p> We gather this year on the 64th anniversary of the independence of Tanzania , the 50th anniversary of the independence of Angola and Mozambique , the 45th anniversary of the independence of Zimbabwe , and the 35th anniversary of the independence of Namibia . It is a moment not just for celebration , but it is also a moment for critical reflection . We must honour the memories of our founding giants Julius Nyerere , Eduardo Mondlane , Samora Machel , Agostino Neto , Sam Nujoma , Robert Mugabe , Joshua Nkomo , Kenneth Kaunda , Oliver Tambo , Nelson Mandela , and many others . <p> Their vision , courage , boldness @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ of a free Southern Africa that we live in today . We also here in South Africa remember the recently departed David Dabede Mabuza , former President of the Republic of South Africa , and the African National Congress . <p> --&gt; <p> We are also pleased that we have amongst us fraternal parties from across the world . Our liberation movements are under siege from within and without . <p> Most of our movements are having to contend with declining electoral support , shifting demographics , frustrated young people in our various countries , economic inequality , and foreign interference . These trends speak to a deeper crisis , the disconnect between our founding narratives and the lived realities of a new generation of young people . It is a generation that sees and appreciates less of our past victories and more of our present shortcomings . <p> It is a generation that demands jobs , justice , dignity , housing , education , health , and security , and sees our movements as distant , rigid , and slow to adapt .
